finding the config file (neither or both → exit 4); state kept only under `.troupe/`; dotted paths where digit segments index lists; `VALUE` parsed as a YAML 1.2 flow node; scalar printing rules; exit codes and messages |\n| Includes | `file:` YAML includes are embedded in place; other targets become text values; recursive; missing target or cycle → exit 4 |\n| `show` | Prints a scalar; a mapping or list → exit 5 |\n| `set` placement | Existing values change in the file that holds them; text includes keep CRLF line endings; new keys go to the deepest existing ancestor mapping; appending at `index == length`; replacing a whole include → exit 3 |\n| `set` formatting | Quoting style; double-quote escaping; block vs flow collections; literal blocks; folded re-wrapping; new entries placed below trailing comments; end-of-line comment columns |\n| `history` | Per-leaf events as JSON Lines; first command imports; `set` and `remove` events; optional path prefix filter |\n\n**Constraints:** Python 3.12+ with uv; the `eventsourcing` library, persisted under `.troupe/`; Linux/macOS; atomic writes.\n\n**Out of contract (not built):** anchors, aliases and merge keys; assistant: I've loaded the charter's plan context. The riskiest scenario is User Story 2: after `set`, the only bytes that change are the ones the contract requires (FR-014 to FR-020). How we write back decides almost everything else, so I'm asking this first.\n\n**What I found:** the charter (TROUPE-001) mentions "format-preserving YAML round-tripping". But a ruamel.yaml load-modify-dump is only *mostly* format-preserving. It normalises indentation, can re-flow long lines and folded scalars, and rewrites quoting, spacing in flow collections and comment spacing. Several contract rules can't be met that way: folded paragraphs keeping their bytes, comments moved back to their previous column, and new entries placed below trailing comments.\n\n**Options:**\n- **(A) Recommended: find the node, then replace only its text.**\n  - A parser locates the exact character span of the target node (start/end marks, block or flow, quoting style, indicator, end-of-line comment column).\n  - A small set of edit rules builds only the replacement text: one rule each for quoting, block/flow collections, literal/folded blocks, entry placement and comment columns.\n  - That text is spliced into the original file text, and the file is written atomically (temp file + rename).\n  - The document is never re-serialised, so untouched bytes are identical by construction.\n  - This still meets TROUPE-001's intent, and I'd record the reasoning in `research.md`.\n- **(B) ruamel.yaml round-trip dump.** Less code, but it predictably breaks NFR-001 on real files and would need fix-ups that amount to (A) anyway.\n- **(C) Something else.**\n\nReply A, B or C. After that I have one more question, about how history is stored under `.troupe/`, then I'll give you an Engineering Alignment summary to confirm.\n\nWAITING_FOR_PLANNING_INPUT

assistant: Recorded (A): find the node's span, splice in only the new text, never re-serialise the file, and write atomically.\n\n**Last question: where history is stored.** The contract allows state only under `<theatre>/.troupe/` and requires the `eventsourcing` library. The library ships with an SQLite persistence module, selected by configuration rather than code.\n\n- **(A) Recommended: the library's built-in SQLite storage.**\n  - One database file, `<theatre>/.troupe/history.sqlite`.\n  - One history record per theatre. Each command appends events for that run, and `seq` is the event's position in the history (1, 2, …).\n  - It's the real library storage the charter's testing rule (TROUPE-005) asks for: no custom serialisation, and an interrupted run can't leave half its events written.\n  - The database's temporary files (`-journal`/`-wal`) also stay inside `.troupe/`.\n- **(B) A custom JSON-file store** built on the library's in-memory storage. Easier to read by eye, but it adds serialisation code and risks a partial write.\n- **(C) Something else.**\n\nReply A, B or C. Then I'll send the Engineering Alignment summary for you to confirm before I write `plan.md`.\n\nWAITING_FOR_PLANNING_INPUT

assistant: Both answers are recorded and the decision check is clean. Here is the engineering plan I'll write `plan.md` from. Please confirm it or correct it.\n\n**Engineering Alignment**\n\n- **Stack:** Python 3.12 managed with uv, with a `troupe` console script so `uv run troupe config …` works from the repository root. Quality gates: pytest, `mypy --strict` and ruff.\n- **Parts, each with one job:**\n  1. **`cli`**: parses arguments and maps each error to its exit code (2, 3, 4 or 5). On failure stdout is empty and stderr names the path or file.\n  2. **`loader`**: reads the root file, follows includes recursively and catches missing targets and cycles (exit 4). It builds a tree where every node records its source file and position, and reads the files fresh on every command, because files are the source of truth (FR-008).\n  3. **`paths` / `values`**: dotted-path lookup, parsing `VALUE` as YAML 1.2 core, and the scalar printing rules.\n  4. **`writeback`**: the find-and-splice editor. It works out where the change goes (FR-010 to FR-013) and builds only the replacement text (quoting, new collections, literal and folded blocks, entry placement, comment columns). It splices that text into the original file and writes the file atomically.\n  5. **`history`**: built on `eventsourcing`, with one record per theatre stored in SQLite at `.troupe/history.sqlite`. It handles the one-time import, the per-leaf `set`/`remove` events and the JSON Lines output.\n- **Order inside `set`:** load, then resolve where the change goes (a refusal changes nothing), then splice and write the file, then compare leaves before and after and append the events. If the write fails, no events are recorded. A `set` that changes nothing writes nothing and records nothing.\n- **Testing:** black-box tests that run the real CLI against temporary theatres. Each `set` test compares the whole file byte for byte against the expected output, and history runs against the real SQLite store.\n- **Choices I'm making and will record in `research.md`:**\n  - A text include's file encoding and its CRLF line endings are detected from its existing bytes.\n  - The file's indentation is taken from its first indented block, defaulting to 2.\n  - Paths of new events use the merged configuration tree, not the file each value came from.\n\nReply "confirmed" or send corrections, and I'll generate `plan.md`, `research.md`, `data-model.md`, `contracts/` and `quickstart.md`.\n\nWAITING_FOR_PLANNING_INPUT

tool: Write {"content": "# Research: troupe configuration: load, show, set\n\nEach entry: Decision · Rationale · Alternatives considered. Product-owner decisions are cited by id;\nthe rest are implementer choices where the contract is silent (charter TROUPE-008/009).\n\n## R-01 Write-back technique — span splice, never re-serialise (DM-01M3XGW5Z97R8VC909JF3TVPTT)\n\n- **Decision**: compose each file with `ruamel.yaml` to get nodes with `start_mark`/`end_mark`\n  (code-point `index`, `line`, `column`), scalar `style` (`None` plain, `'`, `\"`, `|`, `>`) and\n  collection `flow_style`. Renderers produce only the replacement text for the affected span(s);\n  the result is `old_text[:start] + new + old_text[end:]`. Line-level context (indentation,\n  end-of-line comment) is read from the original text, not from the parser.\n- **Rationale**: untouched bytes are identical by construction, which is what FR-014 requires and\n  what users reviewing diffs need. Verified locally: ruamel 0.19.1 compose gives exact indices and\n  styles (e.g. `a: \"x\"  # c` → `\"` style, span 3–6).\n- **Alternatives**: ruamel round-trip load/modify/dump — rejected by the product owner; it normalises\n  indentation, quoting, flow spacing, folded scalars and comment spacing. tool: Bash {"command": "cat >> spec/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG/research.md <<'EOF'\n\n## R-13 ruamel's 1.2 resolver is not the YAML 1.2 core schema (found during /method-command)\n\n- **Finding**: `YAML(typ=\"safe\", pure=True)` (default version 1.2) resolves `2001-12-14` to a\n  `date`, `1_000` to `1000`, and treats `=`/`<<` specially — none of which the YAML 1.2 core schema\n  does.\n- **Decision**: use ruamel only to **compose** (nodes, marks, styles, already-unescaped scalar text).\n  Resolve plain scalars with our own core-schema rules in one place, `src/troupe/yamlcore.py`:\n  null `null|Null|NULL|~|<empty>`; bool `true|True|TRUE|false|False|FALSE`; int `[-+]?[0-9]+`,\n  `0o[0-7]+`, `0x[0-9a-fA-F]+`; float `[-+]?(\\.[0-9]+|[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]*)?)([eE][-+]?[0-9]+)?`,\n  `[-+]?\\.(inf|Inf|INF)`, `\\.(nan|NaN|NAN)`; everything else a string. Quoted and block scalars are\n  always strings. `yamlcore.py` is the only module importing ruamel (mypy/typing boundary).\n\n## R-14 Byte-order mark\n\n- **Finding**: ruamel mark indices count a leading U+FEFF as one character.\n- **Decision**: decode files with plain `utf-8` (not `utf-8-sig`), so the BOM stays in the text as a\n  character; indices align with the text and writing `text.encode(\"utf-8\")` preserves the BOM.\n  Supersedes the BOM note in R-05.\n\n## R-15 eventsourcing application lifetime\n\n- **Finding**: an `Application` whose last reference is dropped closes its SQLite connection pool\n  (`ConnectionPoolClosedError` on a temporary). Versions start at 1 with the creation event; WAL\n  mode creates `history.sqlite-wal`/`-shm` beside the database (inside `.troupe/`).\n- **Decision**: one `HistoryStore` instance per command held by the session context manager and\n  closed explicitly; `seq = originator_version - 1`.\nEOF"}

tool: Bash {"command": "python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport pathlib\np=pathlib.Path(\"tasks/WP02-loader-includes-paths.md\"); s=p.read_text()\nold=\"  - `read_source(path: Path) -> SourceFile`: `path.read_bytes().decode(\\\"utf-8\\\")` (strict). `UnicodeDecodeError` → `LoadError` naming the file. **Never** use text-mode reading (it would translate CRLF).\"\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old, old+\"\"\"\n  - Public entry point: `load(config_file: Path, overrides: Mapping[Path, str] | None = None) -> Node`. When a resolved path is in `overrides`, use that text instead of reading the disk. WP08 uses this to re-load the edited texts **in memory** and verify an edit before writing anything (research R-17). Test it.\"\"\")\np.write_text(s)\nEOF\ncat >> research.md <<'EOF'\n\n## R-16 Text include with a non-string VALUE (contract leaves a choice open)\n\n- **Contract**: \"For a text include, the target file's content becomes `VALUE` exactly, except that if\n  the target file used CRLF line endings, the new content uses CRLF line endings too (`VALUE` must\n  then be a string, else exit 3).\"\n- **Decision**: CRLF target + non-string VALUE → exit 3 (as written). LF target + non-string\n  **scalar** VALUE → content is the scalar's canonical YAML text (`5`, `true`, `null`, `0.2`), which\n  is what \"becomes VALUE exactly\" yields for a text file; a mapping/list VALUE → exit 3 (a text file\n  cannot hold a collection value). \"Uses CRLF\" = the target text contains `\\r\\n`.\n- **Rationale**: follows the contract literally where it speaks; refuses (TROUPE-002) only where a\n  value cannot be represented as text.\n\n## R-17 Verify before write\n\n- **Decision**: `set` builds all new file texts, re-loads the configuration **in memory** from them\n  (`loader.load(..., overrides=...)`), and checks that (a) the value at PATH equals the new value\n  (type-aware) and (b) every other leaf is unchanged except those under PATH. If not, the command\n  refuses with exit 3 (\"cannot write PATH byte-surgically\")...[truncated 337 chars]

No code has been written yet.\n\n**Branch contract:** planning, base and method merge target are all `feat/troupe-config-load-show-set`, and the current branch matches. After acceptance, it is merged into `main` locally, with no push and no PR.\n\n**Commits on that branch:** `4f345c4` (requirement mapping), `cc97760` (tasks, from `finalize-tasks`), `4ba6222` (agent profiles plus the updates to `research.md` and `plan.md`). method's `status.events.jsonl` and `status.json` aren't committed; they're the tool's own bookkeeping.\n\n**Work packages** (all prompts are in `spec/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG/tasks/`):\n\n| WP | Goal | Subtasks | Lines | Depends on |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| WP01 | Project setup, CLI, exit codes, finding the root file | 5 | 259 | — |\n| WP02 | Loader, includes, paths, YAML 1.2 core resolver | 5 | 198 | WP01 |\n| WP03 | `eventsourcing` history, import on first command, `history` | 5 | 204 | WP02 |\n| WP04 | `VALUE` parsing, scalar printing, `show` (User Story 1) | 4 | 132 | WP02, WP03 |\n| WP05 | Atomic writes, quoting and escaping, comment columns | 5 | 192 | WP02 |\n| WP06 | Literal and folded block scalars | 5 | 164 | WP05 |\n| WP07 | New keys and items, flow appends, replacing one kind of node with another | 6 | 184 | WP05, WP06 |\n| WP08 | `set` end to end, with byte-for-byte integration tests (User Story 2) | 7 | 200 | WP02–WP07 |\n\n- **Size:** 42 subtasks, 132 to 259 lines per prompt (about 190 on average). Nothing goes over the limits (more than 10 subtasks or 700 lines), and every prompt opens with the "Do This First: Load Agent Profile" section. All 8 use `python-pedro` (the Python implementer profile).\n- **Parallel work:** after WP02, the history and `show` branch (WP03 → WP04) can run alongside the write-back branch (WP05 → WP06 → WP07). No two WPs own the same file.\n- **Smallest useful slice:** WP01 → WP04 gives working `show` and `history` commands.\n\n**Findings from checking the libraries, recorded in `research.md` (R-13 to R-17):**\n- **YAML 1.2 core values:** ruamel's "1.2" mode is not strict YAML 1.2 core; `2001-12-14` becomes a date and `1_000` becomes 1000. Plain values will use our own core-schema resolver in `yamlcore.py`, the only module that imports ruamel.\n- **Byte-order mark:** files are decoded as plain UTF-8 so a byte-order mark stays in the text and is written back unchanged.\n- **History store lifetime:** one history store object is kept open for each command, because the library closes its SQLite connection when the object is dropped.\n- **Verify before writing:** `set` reloads the edited text in memory before writing. If PATH doesn't hold the new value, or any other value changed, it refuses with exit 3 and changes no file, so a bug can't corrupt a file.\n\n**Decisions I made where the contract is open — please check:**\n- **Text include, non-string `VALUE`:** if the target uses CRLF, exit 3, as the contract says. If it uses plain LF line endings, the content becomes the value's YAML text (`5`, `true`). A mapping or list is refused with exit 3.\n- **Replacing a flow collection:** replacing `tools: [a, b]` (a list written inline) under a normal block mapping writes the new list in block style. I read the "new collection value" rule literally, so the line changes shape.\n- **Refusals on edge cases:** these exit 3 or 5 rather than guess:\n  - a `key:` with no value, treated as null rather than a mapping;\n  - changing a folded value that already has more-indented lines;\n  - a path like `tools.2.name` that continues past a list append.\n\n**Next step:** `/method-command` is required before implementation. The implement step won't start until its `analysis-report.md` exists.\n\n**Should I use the `/method-implement-review` skill to implement all WPs to completion?** It would run `/method-command` first, then implement and review every WP, handle rejections, merge all lanes, and finally merge into `main` locally. You can also pick which models implement and review, choose a subset (say, WP01–WP04 first), or run `/method-command` alone to look at the findings first.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-a && cat > src/troupe/errors.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Error hierarchy and the single place where CLI exit codes are decided.\n\nEvery contracted failure is a ``TroupeError`` subclass whose ``exit_code`` is the code the CLI\nreturns. Constructors take the complete, user-facing message; callers format it, and it must\nname the offending configuration path (e.g. ``model.temperature``) or file (absolute or\ntheatre-relative path), so the user can see where the problem is (TROUPE-003).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import ClassVar\n\n\nclass TroupeError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base for every contracted failure; carries the user-facing message.\"\"\"\n\n    exit_code: ClassVar[int] = 1\n\n    def __init__(self, message: str) -> None:\n        super().__init__(message)\n        self.message = message\n\n\nclass UsageError(TroupeError):\n    \"\"\"Malformed invocation or unparsable VALUE.\"\"\"\n\n    exit_code = 2\n\n\nclass WriteRefused(TroupeError):\n    \"\"\"A write was refused and no file was changed.\"\"\"\n\n    exit_code = 3\n\n\nclass LoadError(TroupeError):\n    \"\"\"The configuration cannot be loaded.\"\"\"\n\n    exit_code = 4\n\n\nclass PathError(TroupeError):\n    \"\"\"A configuration path was not found or names the wrong kind of node.\"\"\"\n\n    exit_code = 5\nEOF\ncat > src/troupe/theatre.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Theatre and root-file discovery (FR-002).\n\nA theatre is a directory whose configuration lives in exactly one of\n``<theatre>/.troupe/troupe.yml`` or ``<theatre>/.troupe/troupe.yaml``. Discovery only reads the\nfilesystem; it never creates anything.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\n\nSTATE_DIR_NAME = \".troupe\"\nROOT_FILE_NAMES = (\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\")\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Theatre:\n    \"\"\"A located theatre.\n\n    Attributes:\n        directory: The theatre directory (resolved, a...[truncated 5323 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b && cat > /tmp/probe.py <<'EOF'\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\nimport io\ndef dump(n, t, d=0):\n    s,e=n.start_mark.index,n.end_mark.index\n    print(\"  \"*d, type(n).__name__, getattr(n,'style',None), getattr(n,'flow_style',None), (s,e), repr(t[s:e]) if type(n).__name__=='ScalarNode' else repr(t[s:e][:40]), repr(n.value) if type(n).__name__=='ScalarNode' else \"\")\n    if type(n).__name__=='MappingNode':\n        for k,v in n.value: dump(k,t,d+1); dump(v,t,d+1)\n    elif type(n).__name__=='SequenceNode':\n        for v in n.value: dump(v,t,d+1)\nfor t in [\"\\ufeffa: 1\\r\\nb: 'x'  # c\\r\\n\", \"a:\\n  b: |\\n    hi\\n\\n  c: >-\\n    x\\n    y\\n\\nd:\\n  - 1\\n  - [2, {e: f}]\\n# tail\\n\", \"\", \"# only comment\\n\", \"a: 1\\n---\\nb: 2\\n\", \"a: &x 1\\nb: *x\\n\", \"a: !t 1\\n\", \"a: [1,\\n\", \"\\\"q\\\\tx\\\": 1\\n\", \"a:\\nb: ~\\n\", \"- a\\n- b\"]:\n    print(repr(t))\n    y=YAML(typ=\"safe\", pure=True)\n    try:\n        n=y.compose(t)\n        if n is None: print(\"None\"); continue\n        dump(n,t)\n    except Exception as ex: print(\"ERR\", type(ex).__mro__, ex)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe.py", "description": "Probe ruamel compose marks"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b && cat > /tmp/probe2.py <<'EOF'\nexec(open('/tmp/probe.py').read().split('for t in')[0])\nfor t in [\"a: plain\\n  multi  # c\\nb: 'q\\n  x'\\nc: {}\\nd: []\\ne:\\n- \\n- x\\nf:\", \"a: &x\\n  b: 1\\nc: !!str 1\\n\", \"a: 1\\na: 2\\n\", \"[a]: 1\\n\", \"? a\\n: 1\\n\", \"a: \\x00\\n\", \"a:\\n  - b\\n\\n\\n# c\\n\\nz: 1\\n\", \"a: |+\\n  x\\n\\n\\nb: 1\\n\", \"a: |\\n  x\\n# c\\nb: 1\\n\"]:\n    print(repr(t))\n    y=YAML(typ=\"safe\", pure=True)\n    try:\n        n=y.compose(t)\n        if n is None: print(\"None\"); continue\n        dump(n,t)\n        print(\"anchor\", getattr(n,'anchor','NA'), [ (getattr(v,'anchor','NA'), v.tag, getattr(v,'ctag','NA')) for k,v in n.value])\n    except Exception as ex: print(\"ERR\", type(ex).__name__, getattr(ex,'problem',None), getattr(ex,'problem_mark',None) and ex.problem_mark.line, '|', getattr(ex,'context',None))\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe2.py 2>&1 | grep -v hardlink", "description": "Probe more ruamel edge cases"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"SourceTree node types: the merged configuration, where every node knows its source span.\n\nThe tree is rebuilt from the files on disk by ``troupe.loader.load`` for every command. Each node\ncarries an ``Origin``: the ``SourceFile`` that contains its bytes and the code-point span\n``file.text[start:end]`` it was parsed from. A node reached through a YAML include belongs to the\nincluded file. This module is stdlib-only so that every other module can depend on it.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Literal\n\ntype Scalar = str | int | float | bool | None\ntype ScalarStyle = Literal[\"plain\", \"single\", \"double\", \"literal\", \"folded\"]\n\n\n@dataclass(eq=False)\nclass SourceFile:\n    \"\"\"One file read from disk (identity semantics: one object per resolved path per load).\n\n    Attributes:\n        path: Absolute, resolved path.\n        text: Strict UTF-8 decoded text with original newlines; a leading BOM stays as U+FEFF, so\n            parser indices and ``text`` agree character for character (research R-14).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    path: Path\n    text: str\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Origin:\n    \"\"\"Where a node's bytes live: ``file.text[start:end]`` (code-point indices, end exclusive).\n\n    Spans are recorded exactly as the parser reports them; see ``troupe.yamlcore`` for where the\n    ends of block scalars, block collections and empty values land.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    file: SourceFile\n    start: int\n    end: int\n\n    @property\n    def source(self) -> str:\n        \"\"\"The source text the span covers.\"\"\"\n        return self.file.text[self.start : self.end]\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ScalarNode:\n    \"\"\"A scalar; ``value`` is resolved per the YAML 1.2 core schema (plain) or a string.\"\"\"\n\n    origin: Origin\n    value: Scalar\n    style: ScalarStyle\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass MappingEntry:\n    \"\"\"One ``key: value`` pair; ``key`` is the key text as written (une...[truncated 1374 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The one place that knows the YAML library (ruamel.yaml) and the YAML 1.2 core schema.\n\nNo other module imports ``ruamel``: ``compose`` turns ruamel's node graph into the small typed\n``Raw*`` dataclasses below, and ``resolve_plain`` resolves plain scalars ourselves, because\nruamel's own resolver is not the YAML 1.2 core schema (research R-13).\n\nSpan behaviour (ruamel 0.19, verified by ``tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py``). ``start``/``end`` are\ncode-point indices into the composed string; a leading BOM is index 0 and CRLF counts as two.\nSpans are recorded exactly as ruamel reports them; consumers that need precise ends of block\nnodes must derive them from the text:\n\n- Plain scalars: the span covers the scalar text only; trailing spaces and comments are excluded.\n  A multi-line plain scalar's span covers all its lines.\n- Quoted scalars: the span includes the quotes.\n- Block scalars (``|``, ``>``): the span starts at the indicator and ends after the content\n  *including* the trailing line breaks the scalar consumes (e.g. blank lines kept by ``|+`` or\n  stripped by clip/``-``); comment lines after the content are not included.\n- Empty (null) values are zero-width spans whose position is unreliable: ``a:`` followed by a\n  newline yields the start of the *next* line, ``- `` yields the column after the dash, and a\n  value at end of file yields ``len(text)``.\n- Block collections: ``start`` is the first key/dash; ``end`` is the start of the next token\n  after the collection, so it usually lies past trailing blank lines *and comment lines* (at the\n  start of the next sibling's line, or at end of file).\n- Flow collections: the span is exactly ``[...]`` / ``{...}``.\n- Anchors and tags would be inside a node's span; they are rejected here (out of contract, C-004).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport re\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\nfrom ruamel.yaml.error import MarkedYAMLError, YAMLErr...[truncated 4660 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/yamlcore.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''    if start < len(text) and text[start] in \"&!*\":\n        line = text.count(\"\\\\n\", 0, start) + 1\n        raise LoadError(f\"cannot parse {source}: anchors, aliases and tags are not supported\"\n                        f\" at line {line}\")\n'''\nnew='''    if end > start and text[start] in \"&!\":  # a plain/quoted/collection node cannot start so\n        line = text.count(\"\\\\n\", 0, start) + 1\n        raise LoadError(f\"cannot parse {source}: anchors and tags are not supported at line {line}\")\n'''\nassert old in s\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF", "description": "Refine anchor/tag check"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Build the SourceTree from the files on disk: root file plus recursive includes (FR-007, FR-008).\n\nFiles are read fresh on every call (no caching across runs), as bytes decoded with strict UTF-8,\nnever in text mode, so CRLF and a BOM stay in ``SourceFile.text`` and parser indices match it\n(research R-05, R-14).\n\nInclude rule (the one place it is decided): a string value of any style whose text starts with\n``file:`` names a target relative to the directory of the (resolved) file containing it. A target\nending ``.yml``/``.yaml`` (case-sensitive) is a ``YamlInclude`` whose content is embedded in\nplace; any other target is a ``TextInclude`` whose value is the file's exact text. Mapping keys\nare never includes.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom troupe import yamlcore\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\nfrom troupe.tree import (\n    MappingEntry,\n    MappingNode,\n    Node,\n    Origin,\n    ScalarNode,\n    SequenceNode,\n    SourceFile,\n    TextInclude,\n    YamlInclude,\n)\nfrom troupe.yamlcore import RawMapping, RawNode, RawScalar\n\nINCLUDE_PREFIX = \"file:\"\nYAML_SUFFIXES = (\".yml\", \".yaml\")\nBOM = \"﻿\"\n\n\ndef read_source(path: Path) -> SourceFile:\n    \"\"\"Read ``path`` as strict UTF-8 without newline translation.\n\n    Raises:\n        LoadError: If the file cannot be read or is not valid UTF-8; the message names the file.\n    \"\"\"\n    resolved = path.resolve()\n    try:\n        data = resolved.read_bytes()\n    except OSError as err:\n        raise LoadError(f\"cannot read {resolved}: {err.strerror or err}\") from err\n    try:\n        text = data.decode(\"utf-8\")\n    except UnicodeDecodeError as err:\n        raise LoadError(f\"cannot read {resolved}: not valid UTF-8 at byte {err.start}\") from err\n    return SourceFile(path=resolved, text=text)\n\n\ndef load(config_file: Path, overrides: Mapping[Path, str] | None = None) -> Node:\n    \"\"\"Load the configuration...[truncated 5646 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b && sed -i 's/^BOM = .*/BOM = \"\\\\ufeff\"/' src/troupe/loader.py && grep -n '^BOM' src/troupe/loader.py | cat -A | head -2", "description": "Use escaped BOM constant"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Configuration paths (FR-003): ``.``-separated segments; a digit-only segment indexes a list.\n\nWalking is include-aware: a ``YamlInclude`` is transparent and the walk continues at its\n``root``. ``Walk.nodes`` deliberately holds the node *as found* at each step (not unwrapped), so\na caller can tell that the whole value at a path comes from a YAML include\n(``isinstance(walk.nodes[-1], YamlInclude)``); use ``unwrap`` to get the embedded content.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n\nfrom troupe.errors import PathError, UsageError\nfrom troupe.tree import MappingNode, Node, SequenceNode, YamlInclude\n\n\ndef parse_path(text: str) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Split ``\"a.b.0\"`` into ``[\"a\", \"b\", \"0\"]``.\n\n    Raises:\n        UsageError: If any segment is empty (including the empty path); the message names it.\n    \"\"\"\n    segments = text.split(\".\")\n    if \"\" in segments:\n        raise UsageError(f\"invalid path '{text}': empty segment\")\n    return segments\n\n\ndef is_index(segment: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return whether ``segment`` is digit-only (ASCII ``0-9``), i.e. a list index.\"\"\"\n    return segment.isascii() and segment.isdigit()\n\n\ndef unwrap(node: Node) -> Node:\n    \"\"\"Return the content of ``node``: the (innermost) root of a ``YamlInclude``, else ``node``.\"\"\"\n    while isinstance(node, YamlInclude):\n        node = node.root\n    return node\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Walk:\n    \"\"\"Result of walking a path as far as it exists.\n\n    Attributes:\n        nodes: ``nodes[0]`` is the root; ``nodes[i]`` is the node reached after ``i`` segments,\n            as found (a ``YamlInclude`` is not unwrapped; see ``unwrap``).\n        remaining: Segments not consumed; empty when the whole path exists. The walk stops at a\n            missing mapping key or at a list index ``>= len(list)``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    nodes: list[Node]\n    remaining: list[str]\n\n\ndef walk(root: Node, segments: list[str]) -> Walk:\n   ...[truncated 1941 chars]

/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b/tests/unit/test_yamlcore.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"yamlcore: YAML 1.2 core-schema resolution and the spans ruamel reports (research R-13, R-14).\"\"\"\n\nimport math\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\nfrom troupe.tree import Scalar\nfrom troupe.yamlcore import RawMapping, RawNode, RawScalar, RawSequence, compose, resolve_plain\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"text\", \"expected\"),\n    [\n        (\"null\", None),\n        (\"Null\", None),\n        (\"NULL\", None),\n        (\"~\", None),\n tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b && cat > tests/integration/test_loading.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Loading real theatre files into the SourceTree: values, spans, includes, errors (FR-007/008).\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError\nfrom troupe.loader import load, read_source\nfrom troupe.paths import lookup, unwrap\nfrom troupe.theatre import locate\nfrom troupe.tree import (\n    MappingNode,\n    Node,\n    Origin,\n    ScalarNode,\n    SequenceNode,\n    TextInclude,\n    YamlInclude,\n)\n\n\ndef _load(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, files: dict[str, str | bytes]) -> Node:\n    return load(locate(make_theatre(files)).config_file)\n\n\ndef _at(root: Node, dotted: str) -> Node:\n    return unwrap(lookup(root, dotted.split(\".\"), dotted))\n\n\ndef _scalar(root: Node, dotted: str) -> ScalarNode:\n    node = _at(root, dotted)\n    assert isinstance(node, ScalarNode)\n    return node\n\n\ndef _origins(node: Node) -> Iterator[Origin]:\n    \"\"\"Every key and scalar/include origin in document order, following YAML includes.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(node, MappingNode):\n        for entry in node.entries:\n            yield entry.key_origin\n            yield from _origins(entry.value)\n    elif isinstance(node, SequenceNode):\n        for item in node.items:\n            yield from _origins(item)\n    elif isinstance(node, YamlInclude):\n        yield node.origin\n        yield from _origins(node.root)\n    else:\n        yield node.origin\n\n\ndef _sources(root: Node) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:\n    return [(o.file.path.name, o.file.text[o.start : o.end]) for o in _origins(root)]\n\n\nNESTED = (\n    \"# troupe\\n\"\n    \"model:\\n\"\n    \"  name: \\\"gpt\\\"   # quoted\\n\"\n    \"  temperature: 0.2\\n\"\n    \"  stop: ~\\n\"\n    \"  enabled: yes\\n\"\n    \"tools:\\n\"\n    \"  - tool: Bash {"command": "cat > /tmp/align-lane.sh <<'EOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Align a lane worktree's spec/ index with the planning branch so method's\n# \"no spec changes on lane branches\" guard passes. Only touches spec/.\nset -euo pipefail\nL=\"$1\"; P=feat/troupe-config-load-show-set\ncd \"$L\"\nfind . -name __pycache__ -not -path './.venv/*' -prune -exec rm -rf {} + 2>/dev/null || true\nchanged=$(git diff --name-only \"$P\" HEAD -- spec/ || true)\n[ -z \"$changed\" ] && { echo \"already aligned\"; exit 0; }\nwhile IFS= read -r f; do\n  if git cat-file -e \"$P:$f\" 2>/dev/null; then\n    git restore --source \"$P\" --staged -- \"$f\"\n    git show \"$P:$f\" > /dev/null\n  else\n    git rm -q --cached --sparse -- \"$f\"\n  fi\ndone <<< \"$changed\"\ngit commit -q -m \"chore: align lane spec with planning branch (method lane guard)\"\n# make worktree match index for spec (avoid dirty-tree refusals)\ngit checkout -- spec/ 2>/dev/null || true\ngit status --short -- spec/ | head\necho \"aligned: $(git log --oneline -1)\"\nEOF\nchmod +x /tmp/align-lane.sh; /tmp/align-lane.sh /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b; git -C .worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b diff --name-only feat/troupe-config-load-show-set HEAD -- spec/; git -C .worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-b status --short | head"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Byte-surgical write-back: never re-serialise a file, splice only the replacement text.\n\nDecision DM-01M3XGW5Z97R8VC909JF3TVPTT (research R-01): renderers look at the original text and\nthe spans recorded in the SourceTree and return ``TextEdit`` values, each replacing one span of a\n``SourceFile.text`` with new text. ``apply_edits`` splices them into the original text, so every\nbyte outside the edited spans is identical by construction. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Newline handling and atomic file replacement (research R-05, R-12, R-14; NFR-002).\n\nTexts are always written as UTF-8 bytes, never through text mode, so CRLF line endings and a\nleading BOM (kept as U+FEFF in ``SourceFile.text``) reach the disk exactly as they are in the text.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport contextlib\nimport os\nimport stat\nimport tempfile\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom troupe.tree import SourceFile\n\nCRLF = \"\\r\\n\"\nLF = \"\\n\"\n\n\ndef detect_newline(text: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the line ending for new lines: ``\"\\\\r\\\\n\"`` if the first line break is CRLF.\"\"\"\n    first = text.find(LF)\n    return CRLF if first > 0 and text[first - 1] == \"\\r\" else LF\n\n\ndef uses_crlf(text: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return whether ``text`` contains a CRLF line ending anywhere (FR-010 text includes).\"\"\"\n    return CRLF in text\n\n\ndef to_crlf(value: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Turn every ``\"\\\\n\"`` not already preceded by ``\"\\\\r\"`` into ``\"\\\\r\\\\n\"``.\"\"\"\n    return value.replace(CRLF, LF).replace(LF, CRLF)\n\n\ndef atomic_write(path: Path, text: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Replace the existing file ``path`` with ``text`` (UTF-8) all-or-nothing.\n\n    A temporary sibling file is written, flushed and fsynced, given the original's permission\n    bits, then renamed over ``path``. If anything fails, the temporary file is removed and the\n    original is left untouched. ``path`` should be resolved (no symlink), as ``SourceFile.path``\n    is; replacing a symlink would replace the link, not its target.\n\n    Raises:\n        OSError: If the file cannot be written; ``path`` is unchanged.\n    \"\"\"\n    mode = stat.S_IMODE(path.stat().st_mode)\n    fd, tmp_name = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=path.parent, prefix=f\".{path.name}.\", suffix=\".tmp\")\n    try:\n        with os.fdopen(fd, \"wb\") as handle:\n            handle.write(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n            handle.flush()\n          ...[truncated 1231 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Scalar quoting: representability, style choice and rendering (FR-015, research R-10).\n\nContract (FR-015): a changed scalar keeps its quoting style if that style can represent the new\nvalue; otherwise plain if possible, else single-quoted, else double-quoted. A new key or new string\nvalue copies the preceding sibling's style; with no sibling, plain or the minimal quoting the text\nneeds. Callers express both rules through ``preferred`` (the current or sibling style, or\n``None``). A double-quoted scalar escapes only ``\\\\``, ``\"`` and control characters; everything else\nis written literally. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"End-of-line comments on a changed line (FR-020).\n\nContract: when a changed value's length changes, the comment on that line moves back to its\nprevious column; if the new value reaches that column, exactly one space separates value and\ncomment. No other line changes. Columns count Unicode code points, which are Python ``str``\nindices. A TAB before an end-of-line comment is out of contract and not special-cased.\n\"\"\"\n\n_QUOTES = \"'\\\"\"\n# A quote opens a quoted scalar only where a scalar can start; elsewhere (``it's``) it is plain text.\n_SCALAR_STARTS_AFTER = \" \\t,[{:\"\n\n\ndef find_comment_start(line: str, from_col: int) -> int | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the column of the end-of-line comment's ``#`` at or after ``from_col``, or ``None``.\n\n    ``line`` excludes its line break and ``from_col`` must lie outside any quoted scalar. A ``#``\n    outside quotes starts a comment if it is at column 0 or preceded by a space. Inside single\n    quotes ``''`` is an escaped quote; inside double quotes ``\\\\`` escapes the next character.\n    \"\"\"\n    quote: str | None = None\n    i = from_col\n    while i < len(line):\n        ch = line[i]\n        if quote == \"'\":\n            if ch == \"'\":\n                if line[i + 1 : i + 2] == \"'\":\n                    i += 1\n                else:\n                    quote = None\n        elif quote == '\"':\n            if ch == \"\\\\\":\n                i += 1\n            elif ch == '\"':\n                quote = None\n        elif ch in _QUOTES and (i == from_col or line[i - 1] in _SCALAR_STARTS_AFTER):\n            quote = ch\n        elif ch == \"#\" and (i == 0 or line[i - 1] == \" \"):\n            return i\n        i += 1\n    return None\n\n\ndef replace_in_line(line: str, start_col: int, end_col: int, new_text: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Replace ``line[start_col:end_col]`` with ``new_text``, keeping the comment's column.\n\n    Only spaces between the edited text's end of line conten...[truncated 708 chars]

T011 to T015 are marked done; I did not run move-task and did not push.\n\n**Files** (all under `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-c/`). Nothing was changed under `spec/` or `spec/`, and `__pycache__` was removed.\n- `src/troupe/leaves.py`\n- `src/troupe/history.py`\n- `src/troupe/session.py`\n- `src/troupe/commands/history.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_leaves.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_history_store.py`\n- `tests/integration/test_history.py`\n\n**Gate outputs** (final run, in the worktree)\n```\nuv run pytest              -> 184 passed in 7.37s\nuv run mypy --strict       -> Success: no issues found in 22 source files\nuv run ruff check          -> All checks passed!\nuv run ruff format --check -> 25 files already formatted\n```\nI also broke the code on purpose twice to check the tests catch it, then restored it. Creating the store before loading the config made `test_load_failure_creates_no_history_file` fail. Removing the environment override made `test_environment_variables_cannot_redirect_the_store` fail.\n\n**Deviations from the WP prompt, with reasons**\n1. **The store ignores the user's environment variables completely.** The prompt says passing `env=` explicitly is enough. In eventsourcing 9.5.5 it is not: the library still merges `os.environ` and checks name-prefixed keys first (e.g. `_HISTORYAPPLICATION_SQLITE_DBNAME`). Variables like `CIPHER_TOPIC` or `COMPRESSOR_TOPIC` would also leak through and change the file format. So `history.py` uses a private `_HistoryApplication` subclass that overrides the public `construct_env` hook to use only the settings we pass. This is the safe-for-user-data choice (TROUPE-002), and a test covers it.\n2. **The event classes are declared explicitly.** `LeafImported`, `LeafSet` and `LeafRemoved` are declared as nested `Aggregate.Event` subclasses and passed to `@event(...)`, instead of `@event("Name")` strings. That lets `records()` use `isinstance` and stay fully typed under strict mypy; the event names are unchanged. `Created` still uses `@event("Created")`.\n3. **`history` checks PATH before loading.** It calls `parse_path` before opening the session, so a bad PATH exits 2 and creates nothing, which research R-04 requires ("usage errors occur before loading"). A test checks this.\n4. **Extra tests beyond the list:**\n   - non-ASCII output stays unescaped\n   - an empty config still creates the history\n   - the PATH filter keeps the original `seq`\n   - a load failure after import appends nothing\n   - an empty-segment PATH exits 2 and creates no files\n   - the environment override (deviation 1)\n\n**Public API for WP04 and WP08**\n```python\n# troupe.leaves\nJsonScalar = str | int | float | bool | None\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Change:\n    op: Literal["set", "remove"]; path: str; old: JsonScalar; new: JsonScalar\ndef flatten(node: Node, prefix: str = "") -> dict[str, JsonScalar]   # document order\ndef same(a: JsonScalar, b: JsonScalar) -> bool                       # type-aware; nan == nan\ndef diff(before: dict[str, JsonScalar], after: dict[str, JsonScalar]) -> list[Change]\n    # sets in `after` order, then removes in `before` order\n\n# troupe.history\nHISTORY_DB_NAME: Final = "history.sqlite"\nHISTORY_ID: Final = uuid.uuid5(uuid.NAMESPACE_URL, "troupe:config-history")\nclass ConfigHistory(Aggregate): ...   # events Created, LeafImported, LeafSet, LeafRemoved\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HistoryRecord:\n    seq: int; op: Literal["import", "set", "remove"]; path: str; old: JsonScalar; new: JsonScalar\nclass HistoryStore:\n    def __init__(self, state_dir: Path) -> None          # creates the DB file\n    def is_imported(self) -> bool\n    def import_leaves(self, leaves: dict[str, JsonScalar]) -> None   # one save()\n    def record(self, changes: list[Change]) -> None      # [] -> no-op; one save();\n                                                         # AggregateNotFoundError if not imported\n    def records(self) -> list[HistoryRecord]             # oldest first\n    def close(self) -> None\n\n# troupe.session\n@dataclass\nclass Session:\n    theatre: Theatre; tree: Node; history: HistoryStore   # tree = files at command start\n@contextmanager\ndef open_session(theatre: Theatre) -> Iterator[Session]   # load -> open store -> import if needed; closes store\n```\nFor WP08's `set`, the expected flow is:\n1. Open the session with `with open_session(theatre) as s:`.\n2. Take `before = flatten(s.tree)`.\n3. Write, then reload and take `after = flatten(load(theatre.config_file))`.\n4. Call `s.history.record(diff(before, after))` inside the `with` block, because the store is closed on exit.\n\n**Known limit:** `history` prints infinity and NaN values as `Infinity`/`NaN`, which isn't strict JSON. The WP prompt said to accept this, and it is documented in the `history.py` docstring.

assistant: WP05 is implemented and committed as `5a28161` in lane-e. All four gates pass, and T020–T024 are marked done. I did not run move-task and did not push.\n\n**Gates** (run in the worktree):\n```\nuv run pytest            -> 474 passed in 3.40s\nuv run mypy --strict     -> Success: no issues found in 22 source files\nuv run ruff check        -> All checks passed!\nuv run ruff format --check -> 25 files already formatted\n```\nAfter the commit, `git status` shows nothing uncommitted. I deleted the `__pycache__` dirs; the gitignored `.pytest_cache`, `.mypy_cache` and `.ruff_cache` dirs are still there. Nothing under `spec/` or `spec/` was touched.\n\n**Files** (all under `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-e/`):\n- `src/troupe/writeback/__init__.py`\n- `src/troupe/writeback/fileio.py`\n- `src/troupe/writeback/scalars.py`\n- `src/troupe/writeback/comments.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_fileio.py` (also holds the `apply_edits` tests)\n- `tests/unit/test_scalars.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_comments.py`\n\n**Public API for WP06–WP08**\n```python\n# troupe.writeback\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass TextEdit: start: int; end: int; text: str\ndef apply_edits(text: str, edits: Sequence[TextEdit]) -> str   # ValueError on overlap / out of bounds\n\n# troupe.writeback.fileio\ndef detect_newline(text: str) -> str\ndef uses_crlf(text: str) -> bool\ndef to_crlf(value: str) -> str\ndef atomic_write(path: Path, text: str) -> None\ndef write_files(new_texts: Mapping[SourceFile, str]) -> None   # skips files whose text is unchanged\n\n# troupe.writeback.scalars\nContext = Literal["block", "flow"]\nQuoteStyle = Literal["plain", "single", "double"]\ndef can_plain(s: str, context: Context) -> bool\ndef can_single(s: str) -> bool\ndef choose_style(s: str, preferred: QuoteStyle | None, context: Context) -> QuoteStyle\ndef render_string(s: str, style: QuoteStyle) -> str\ndef render_nonstring(v: int | float | bool | None) -> str\ndef render_scalar(v: Scalar, preferred: QuoteStyle | None, context: Context) -> str\n\n# troupe.writeback.comments\ndef find_comment_start(line: str, from_col: int) -> int | None\ndef replace_in_line(line: str, start_col: int, end_col: int, new_text: str) -> str\n```\n- **Insertions at one index:** `apply_edits` keeps their given order, and an insertion right at a replacement's boundary is allowed.\n- **Edit renderers:** callers must turn `literal`/`folded` into a `QuoteStyle` themselves (or pass `None`) before calling `choose_style`/`render_scalar`.\n\n**Deviations from the WP prompt, with reasons**\n1. **`tab\there` with preferred `plain` gives `single`, not `double`.** The prompt's test table said `double`, but that contradicts its own `can_single` rule (tabs allowed) and the contract order "plain, else single, else double". I checked that ruamel reads `'tab	here'` back correctly. Multi-line text is the case that tests `double`.\n2. **Flow comment test input changed.** The prompt's example (`tools: [a, b]  # c`, `b`→`bbb`) can't keep the column: the new value reaches it, so the contract's one-space rule applies. I used `tools: [a, b]    # c`, which shows the column kept, and the reach case has its own test.\n3. **Extra double-quote escapes.** Surrogates and U+FFFE/U+FFFF are written as `\uXXXX`. YAML can't contain them literally, so writing them raw would leave an unreadable file.\n4. **Extra plain refusals.** `can_plain` also refuses text starting with `---` or `...` (document markers), for safety.\n5. **Quote handling in `find_comment_start`.** A quote only opens a quoted scalar where a scalar can start (at `from_col` or after a space, tab, `,`, `[`, `{` or `:`). Otherwise `it's` inside a flow list would hide the comment. This is tested.\n6. **`atomic_write` needs an existing file.** It reads the original's permission bits first, so it is not for creating new files. After the rename it also fsyncs the directory (best effort).\n7. **Round-trip check in `can_plain` has no test that would fail without it.** No input I could find gets past the syntax rules but fails the yamlcore round-trip, so it is a backstop.\n\nNo ruamel import outside `yamlcore`; `scalars` uses `yamlcore.compose` and `resolve_plain`.

assistant: VERDICT: APPROVE\n\nI found no blocking defects in commit `e26cee6`. It does what the history contract asks, and I tried the edge cases myself through the real CLI on throwaway theatres in `/tmp`.\n\n**Quality gates (run in the worktree)**\n- `uv run pytest`: 184 passed. The only baseline failure, the missing JUnit artifact, existed before this work and is unchanged.\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: no issues in 22 source files.\n- `uv run ruff check`: all checks passed.\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: 25 files already formatted.\n\n**Contract checks**\n- **Output format:** `history [PATH]` prints JSON Lines oldest first. Each line has keys in the order `seq, op, path, old, new`, and `seq` starts at 1 (the event version minus 1).\n- **PATH filter:** it matches PATH exactly or anything starting with `PATH.`. Tests cover `model` vs `models` / `model_x` / `model2`. A filtered line keeps its original `seq`. A PATH that matches nothing prints nothing and exits 0. An empty segment exits 2 before anything is loaded, and no file is created.\n- **Import:** it happens only in `session.py:open_session` and only when the history doesn't exist yet. The config is loaded before the store is opened, so a load failure (exit 4) creates no `history.sqlite`; a test covers this, plus a load failure after import adding nothing. Hand edits produce no events, a second run doesn't import again, and an empty theatre still counts as imported.\n- **Leaves:** a YAML include contributes its leaves at the merged paths, including nested includes and an include whose content is a list. A text include is one leaf holding the file's exact text; I confirmed CRLF bytes are kept. List items are `x.0`, `x.1`, and empty collections give no leaves.\n- **`leaves.diff`:**\n  - It compares by type, so `1`, `1.0`, `"1"` and `True` all count as different; NaN counts as equal to NaN.\n  - It compares by presence: a new leaf whose value is null is still a `set`, and a vanished leaf that held null is still a `remove`.\n  - Order is `set`s in `after` order, then `remove`s in `before` order.\n- **Value types through SQLite (tested and checked by hand):** bool, `1.0` as a float, int, null, a 30-digit integer, hex resolved to an int, non-ASCII text and an emoji all come back as the same type and value. `.inf`/`.nan` print as `Infinity`/`NaN`, which the module docstring documents as accepted.\n- **State confinement:** only `.troupe/history.sqlite*` is created and the config bytes are unchanged (tested, and I checked the file list myself).\n- **Real library in tests:** the tests use the real eventsourcing library and a real SQLite file, including reading the history back from a freshly opened store. There are no in-memory stand-ins.\n\n**The `construct_env` override.** It's sound and uses a supported hook. In eventsourcing 9.5.5, `Application.construct_env` is a public method that `__init__` calls. The base version starts from the class-level env, adds the snapshotting flag, then merges `os.environ`, then the explicit `env`. The override returns exactly the env passed in. The two things it drops (the class env and the snapshot flag) aren't used here. Leaving out `os.environ` entirely is stricter than the WP prompt's "explicit env wins", and it's justified: eventsourcing also reads name-prefixed variables and `CIPHER_`/`COMPRESSOR_` settings, which could otherwise redirect the store or change its file format. The only `os.environ` read left in the library is `TZINFO_TOPIC` (event timestamp timezone), which is harmless. There is a test that sets the environment variables and confirms the store isn't redirected.\n\n**Anti-pattern checklist**\n1. Dead code: PASS. `diff`, `Change` and `HistoryStore.record` have no production caller yet, but the WP explicitly hands them to WP08's `set`. Everything else is reached via `cli.py` → `commands/history.py` → `session.py`.\n2. Synthetic-fixture tests: PASS. The integration tests run the CLI as a subprocess and the store tests use real SQLite.\n3. Silent empty returns: PASS. `record([])` doing nothing and `records()` returning `[]` before import are both documented and required.\n4. FR coverage: PASS for FR-021, FR-022, FR-024, NFR-003 and C-002. FR-023 is covered at the `diff`/`record` level; the full `set` command is WP08's job.\n5. Frozen surface: PASS. All 7 changed files are in the WP's `owned_files`.\n6. Locked decision: PASS.\n7. Shared-file ownership: PASS. No changed file is shared with another WP.\n8. Production fragility: PASS. There are no new `raise` statements; `record` only documents that it propagates `AggregateNotFoundError`.\n\n**Non-blocking observations (no action required for WP03)**\n- A config whose root is a plain scalar (e.g. `42`) imports one leaf with path `""`. `history PATH` can't filter to it because an empty PATH exits 2, though unfiltered `history` still lists it. A mapping key containing a dot (`"a.b": x`) produces a path that can't be told apart from a nested one. Both come from the spec's path syntax, not this WP.\n- If two processes run their very first command at the same moment, the loser could in theory hit an integrity error from the store. I ran 6 first commands in parallel and all exited 0 with exactly one import, and the spec doesn't contract concurrency, so this is only worth noting.\n\n**Housekeeping:** I removed the `__pycache__` directories my test run created and edited no files. I didn't run move-task and didn't write a feedback file.\n\nFiles reviewed (in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-c`):\n- `src/troupe/leaves.py`\n- `src/troupe/history.py`\n- `src/troupe/session.py`\n- `src/troupe/commands/history.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_leaves.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_history_store.py`\n- `tests/integration/test_history.py`

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"VALUE arguments and scalar printing: the one place both conversions are decided (FR-004/005).\n\n``parse_value`` turns a ``set`` VALUE argument into a plain Python value. VALUE is exactly one\nYAML 1.2 core-schema *flow* node: plain scalars resolve per ``yamlcore.resolve_plain``, quoted\nscalars are strings, ``{...}`` is a ``dict`` and ``[...]`` a ``list``. Block nodes (``a: 1``,\n``- a``, ``|``/``>`` scalars) are refused, because they cannot be told apart from a mistyped\nargument. ``format_scalar`` is how ``show`` prints a scalar.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom troupe import yamlcore\nfrom troupe.errors import LoadError, UsageError\nfrom troupe.tree import Scalar\nfrom troupe.yamlcore import RawMapping, RawNode, RawScalar\n\ntype Value = Scalar | dict[str, Value] | list[Value]\n\n_BLOCK_SCALAR_STYLES = (\"literal\", \"folded\")\n\n\ndef parse_value(text: str) -> Value:\n    \"\"\"Parse a VALUE argument as one YAML 1.2 core flow node.\n\n    An empty (or comment-only) VALUE is the empty plain scalar, i.e. ``None``.\n\n    Raises:\n        UsageError: If ``text`` is not valid YAML, is not a single flow node, uses anchors,\n            aliases or tags, or has a duplicate or non-scalar mapping key; the message quotes\n            ``text``.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        raw = yamlcore.compose(text, source=f\"VALUE {text!r}\")\n    except LoadError as err:\n        raise UsageError(err.message) from err\n    if raw is None:\n        return None\n    if _is_block(raw):\n        raise UsageError(f\"VALUE is not a single YAML flow node: {text!r}\")\n    return _convert(raw, text)\n\n\ndef _is_block(raw: RawNode) -> bool:\n    # Only the root needs checking: YAML forbids block nodes inside flow collections.\n    if isinstance(raw, RawScalar):\n        return raw.style in _BLOCK_SCALAR_STYLES\n    return not raw.flow\n\n\ndef _convert(raw: RawNode, text: str) -> Value:\n    if isinstance(raw, RawScalar):\n        return yamlcore.resolve_plain(raw.text) if raw.style == \...[truncated 1436 chars]

assistant: VERDICT: REJECT\n\nI'm rejecting WP05 (commit `5a28161`) for one real defect. It never corrupts data, but it breaks the FR-015 rule for picking a quoting style on realistic input. Feedback is written to `/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG/tasks/WP05-writeback-primitives/review-feedback-1.md`.\n\n**Gates (in the lane-e worktree):** `uv run pytest` 474 passed; `mypy --strict` no issues; `ruff check` clean; `ruff format --check` clean.\n\n**What holds up:**\n- **Re-parse safety:** I ran about 140 hostile inputs through `choose_style` and `render_string` in both block and flow context, then read each back through `troupe.yamlcore`. Every one came back as exactly the same string, with no type change. They included everything on your list plus `1_000`, `2001-12-14`, `<<`, NBSP, U+0085/2028, lone surrogates and U+FFFE/FFFF.\n- **Non-string values:** `1e+20`, `-0.0`, `5e-324`, `.inf`, `-.inf` and `.nan` all read back as the same value.\n- **Double-quote escaping:** minimal, and it reads back correctly.\n- **Comment column rule:** correct, including `#` inside quotes, flow lines and CJK columns.\n- **`atomic_write`:** writes bytes, so BOM and CRLF survive. It copies the file mode and removes the temp file whether it succeeds or fails.\n- **`apply_edits`:** rejects overlapping and out-of-range edits.\n\n**Blocking defect (B1).** `can_single` and `can_plain` in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-e/src/troupe/writeback/scalars.py` use Python's `str.isprintable()` to decide which characters are allowed. That is stricter than YAML: it also turns away non-breaking spaces, zero-width characters (including the joiner inside emoji like 👩‍💻) and private-use characters. YAML allows all of these, and `yamlcore` reads them back unchanged in plain and single-quoted form. Research R-10 only rules out line breaks and control characters for single quotes. Results I verified:\n- `choose_style("👩‍💻 dev", None, "block")` gives `double`; the contract requires plain.\n- `choose_style("a\u00a0b", "single", "block")` gives `double`; the contract requires keeping single.\n\nThe fix is a YAML printable-character check, excluding U+0085/2028/2029, plus three tests; the feedback file spells it out.\n\n**The deviations you asked me to evaluate:**\n- **Tab goes to single quotes:** correct under the contract. The WP prompt's example (double quotes) was wrong.\n- **Extra `\uXXXX` escapes for surrogates and U+FFFE/FFFF:** YAML requires these, so it's consistent with "escapes only what YAML requires".\n- **Refusing `---`/`...`, and `-v`/`:x`, as plain:** conservative and approved by the WP prompt, but strictly short of "plain if possible" (`-v` becomes `'-v'`). Noted as non-blocking, possibly worth raising with the planner.\n- **Quote-start rule in `find_comment_start`:** it misses the comment when the scan starts before an unquoted value containing ` '`, e.g. replacing `a` in `[a, x 'y]  # c`. That can't happen in the normal block "value then comment" case; non-blocking.\n- **`atomic_write` needs an existing file:** fits R-12; non-blocking.\n\nI did not run move-task. WP06, WP07 and WP08 depend on WP05 and will need a rebase after the fix. I removed the `__pycache__` folders and `/tmp` scripts I created; the untracked `.method/` in the worktree was already there and I left it alone.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config show PATH``: print the scalar at PATH (FR-009).\n\nA text include is a string (the target file's exact text). A mapping, a list (including a whole\nYAML include) or a missing path is a ``PathError`` (exit 5).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\n\nfrom troupe.errors import PathError\nfrom troupe.paths import lookup, parse_path, unwrap\nfrom troupe.session import open_session\nfrom troupe.theatre import Theatre\nfrom troupe.tree import MappingNode, ScalarNode, SequenceNode, TextInclude\nfrom troupe.values import format_scalar\n\n\ndef run(theatre: Theatre, args: argparse.Namespace) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the printed scalar at ``args.path``.\n\n    Raises:\n        UsageError: If PATH has an empty segment (checked before anything is loaded).\n        LoadError: If the configuration cannot be loaded.\n        PathError: If PATH does not exist or names a mapping or list.\n    \"\"\"\n    path: str = args.path\n    segments = parse_path(path)\n    with open_session(theatre) as session:\n        node = unwrap(lookup(session.tree, segments, path))\n    if isinstance(node, ScalarNode):\n        return format_scalar(node.value)\n    if isinstance(node, TextInclude):\n        return format_scalar(node.target.text)\n    kind = \"mapping\" if isinstance(node, MappingNode) else \"list\"\n    assert isinstance(node, MappingNode | SequenceNode)\n    raise PathError(f\"{path} is a {kind}, not a scalar\")\n", "file_path": "/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-d/src/troupe/commands/show.py"}

Accepting them would mean a dict silently dropping one value. This follows the "refuse rather than guess" rule and matches what the loader already does for files.\n2. **Unparsable VALUE errors quote the VALUE.** The parse error from `yamlcore.compose` (source `VALUE '<text>'`) is turned into a usage error, so stderr reads like `troupe: cannot parse VALUE '[a, b': ...`. Anchors, aliases, tags and multi-document input are also exit 2.\n3. **`show` error wording.** A mapping or list gives `troupe: <path> is a mapping, not a scalar` (or `a list`). A whole YAML include counts as a mapping. Plain scalars go through yamlcore.resolve_plain;\n    # quoted scalars are always str; {..} -> dict (key = key text), [..] -> list.\n    # Empty or comment-only VALUE -> None.\n    # Raises UsageError (exit 2) for a block mapping/sequence at the root, a |/> block\n    # scalar, a parse error, anchors/aliases/tags, or a duplicate/non-scalar key.\n    # Every message quotes the VALUE.\n\ndef format_scalar(value: Scalar) -> str\n    # str: as-is, plus "\n" unless it already ends with "\n"; bool: true/false\n    # (checked before int); None: null; int: str(); float: repr(). Always ends in "\n".\n```\nFor WP08:\n- `Value` is a `type` alias, so it can't be used in `isinstance`. Check against `dict`, `list` or the scalar types instead.\n- `parse_value` does not keep the original argument text, so WP08 must use the raw `args.value` when FR-010 says to write a non-string VALUE to an LF text include "exactly as typed".

assistant: Both blocking items are fixed and committed as `4b6a59b` in the lane-e worktree. All four gates pass, and T020–T024 are marked done. I did not run move-task and did not push.\n\n**B1: printable check** (`/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-e/src/troupe/writeback/scalars.py`)\n- I replaced `str.isprintable()` with a YAML 1.2 printable check. It allows tab, U+0020–U+007E, U+00A0–U+D7FF, U+E000–U+FFFD and U+10000–U+10FFFF.\n- It still refuses U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 and U+FEFF in plain and single-quoted values, so they get escaped in double quotes (`\N`, `\L`, `\P`). I tested how `troupe.yamlcore` reads them back. U+0085 turns into a space. U+2028, U+2029 and U+FEFF come back unchanged when they sit in the middle of a line. But the YAML library underneath yamlcore (ruamel) treats U+2028/2029 as line breaks, so I kept the conservative choice you allowed.\n- `can_plain` uses the same check without tab. It also refuses any leading or trailing whitespace, including NBSP and U+3000. Those values now come out single-quoted instead of double, which the reviewer accepted as defensible.\n- Results: `👩‍💻 dev` gives plain; `a<NBSP>b` with preferred `single` stays single; `a<ZWSP>b` with no preference gives plain. ZWJ, soft hyphen, LRM and private-use characters are now accepted as plain too.\n\n**B2: leading `-`, `?`, `:`**\n- These now start a plain value when the next character isn't a space and the yamlcore round-trip check confirms a plain value with the same text. `-`, `?` and `:` on their own are still refused.\n- `-v` and `:x` give plain in both block and flow context. `?x` gives plain only in block context: in flow, yamlcore reads it as a mapping, so the round-trip check refuses it.\n- `---` and `...` prefixes are still refused (`---x` was added to the refusal tests).\n\n**Tests** (`/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-e/tests/unit/test_scalars.py`)\n- All the cases the feedback lists are in, plus extra refusal cases (`-`, `?`, `:`, `- x`, `? x`, U+2029, leading NBSP, trailing U+3000, DEL, U+FFFE, a surrogate).\n- The property-style tests cover every string of length 1–2, plus 1,500 seeded random strings, over an alphabet of indicators, whitespace, control and printable non-ASCII characters. Every string `can_plain` accepts reads back through yamlcore as the identical plain string, in both block and flow context. Every string `can_single` accepts, once single-quoted, reads back identically in both contexts too. I used plain pytest because `hypothesis` isn't installed, and I didn't add a dependency.\n\n**Gates**\n```\nuv run pytest                 -> 579 passed in 8.13s\nuv run mypy --strict          -> Success: no issues found in 22 source files\nuv run ruff check             -> All checks passed!\nuv run ruff format --check    -> 25 files already formatted\nmethod agent tasks mark-status ... -> ✓ Marked 5 subtasks as done: T020, T021, T022, T023, T024\n```\n\nThe `__pycache__` folders are deleted and the worktree is clean. The commit contains only `scalars.py` and `test_scalars.py`; I didn't touch `spec/` or `spec/`.

A quoted `"0.2"` prints `0.2`.\n- **Multi-line string:** a `|` block prints exactly, with no extra newline added.\n- **Unicode:** UTF-8 output is correct, including the astral character 𝄞 and under `LANG=C`.\n- **Text includes:** a CRLF file comes out as the exact bytes `a\r\nb\r\n`. A file with no final newline gets one `\n` added.\n- **Nested includes:** values two includes deep (`inc.deep.v`) and text includes inside a YAML include (`inc.t`) both resolve.\n- **Lists:** `lst.0`, `lst.2` and `lst.1.b` (a mapping inside a list) all print the right scalar.\n- **Exit 5:** a mapping, a list, a whole YAML include, a nested include mapping, a missing key, an out-of-range index, `lst.-1`, and a path through a scalar all exit 5. Stdout is empty and stderr is one `troupe: ...` line naming the path.\n- **Exit 2:** an empty PATH and `a..b` exit 2.\n- **Exit 4:** having both `troupe.yml` and `troupe.yaml` exits 4, and stderr names both files.\n- **First `show` imports the theatre:** the first `show` created `.troupe/history.sqlite` with 26 import events. A second `show` added no new events.\n\n**`parse_value`** (called directly):\n- **Required examples:** all eleven from the WP prompt give the expected result. That includes the shell form `"line one\nline two\n"` with a literal backslash-n, which becomes a real two-line string.\n- **Block forms are rejected with exit 2:** `a: 1`, `- a`, `|`, `>` and `key: [1]`.\n- **Invalid YAML and out-of-contract input also exit 2:** unterminated quotes or brackets, more than one document, anchors, tags, aliases, duplicate keys and non-scalar keys. The error message quotes VALUE with `repr`, so it stays on one stderr line.\n- **Nested collections:** these convert correctly.\n\n**Code review notes:**\n- `format_scalar` checks `bool` before `int`, so `true` does not print as `1`.\n- `show` parses PATH before opening the session, so a bad PATH creates nothing, and it goes through `open_session`.\n- ruamel is only used through `yamlcore`.\n- Every failure test in `tests/integration/test_show.py` checks that stdout is empty (via `(code, out) == (5, "")`). That is valid YAML for a single node.\n- It also accepts a leading `--- ` document marker (`--- 1` becomes `1`).\n- Error messages name the source as `VALUE '<text>'` rather than just `VALUE`. Both issues from cycle 1 are fixed, and I found no regressions or new defects.\n\n**Gates (run in the worktree):**\n- `uv run pytest`: 579 passed\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: no issues in 22 source files\n- `uv run ruff check`: all checks passed\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: 25 files already formatted\n\n**B1 (printable check) is fixed.** `str.isprintable()` is gone. `can_single` now accepts tab plus every YAML 1.2 printable character, except U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 and U+FEFF. `can_plain` uses the same check without tab, refuses any leading or trailing whitespace, and keeps the existing syntax rules and the round-trip check. Results now match the contract:\n- `👩‍💻 dev` with no preference gives plain.\n- `a<NBSP>b` with preferred single stays single.\n- `a<ZWSP>b` gives plain.\n- `<NBSP>lead` falls back to single.\n\n**B2 (leading `-`, `?`, `:`) is fixed.** These characters are allowed when the next character is not a space and the `troupe.yamlcore` round-trip confirms a plain scalar with the same text:\n- `-v` and `:x` give plain in both block and flow.\n- `?x` gives plain in block and single in flow.\n- `---x`, `- x` and `-1` stay single-quoted. `-1` must be quoted because it would otherwise read back as an integer.\n\n**Adversarial round-trip:** I tried 41,501 strings, each in block and flow context and with every preferred style (None, plain, single, double). The set covered all your listed cases, plus every 1- and 2-character string over a 40-symbol alphabet of tricky characters and 40,000 seeded random strings of length 3 to 8. Every rendered result read back through `troupe.yamlcore` as the identical string in the chosen style, with 0 failures.\n\n**Refusing U+0085/2028/2029/FEFF outside double quotes is acceptable.** For U+0085/2028/2029, writing them literally would change the value, because ruamel treats them as line breaks; inside double quotes they are escaped as `\N`/`\L`/`\P`. For U+FEFF, the refusal is exactly what YAML 1.2 requires: it excludes the byte order mark from the characters allowed in plain and single-quoted scalars, but allows it inside double quotes. One small inaccuracy: the commit message says FEFF "stays escaped in double quotes", but it is actually written literally inside double quotes. That is valid YAML and round-trips, so it is not a defect.\n\n**Regressions:** the fix touches only `src/troupe/writeback/scalars.py` and `tests/unit/test_scalars.py`. Nothing in `src` or `tests` changed after `4b6a59b`. All the tests the cycle-1 feedback asked for are present, including the "every accepted string round-trips" checks for both `can_plain` and `can_single`.\n\n**Non-blocking (no action needed):**\n- I only checked mapping values and flow-sequence items, the contexts this WP defines. If a later WP renders keys through these functions, it should run its own round-trip check for key position.\n- The cycle-1 note about `find_comment_start` (it can wrongly enter quote state on a plain scalar in a flow line) still applies, as it did before.\n\nCleanup: I removed `__pycache__`, `.mypy_cache`, `.ruff_cache` and my temp script in `/tmp/rv`. I made no source or `spec/` edits. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Multi-line strings: literal and folded block scalars (FR-017, FR-018, research R-11).\n\nContract (FR-017): a multi-line string (a ``str`` containing ``\"\\\\n\"``) replacing a plain or quoted\nscalar, or as a new key's value, is written as a literal block (``|``, or ``|-`` without a trailing\nnewline); under a flow parent it is a double-quoted flow scalar. An existing block scalar keeps its\nindicator and chomping unless the value's trailing newlines require a different chomping.\n\nContract (FR-018): in a folded (``>``) value, paragraphs whose text is unchanged keep their bytes; a\nchanged paragraph is re-wrapped greedily by word at the width of its longest old content line (an\nover-long word takes a line of its own); a paragraph with no counterpart in the old value wraps at\nthe value's longest old content line; a change that would introduce a more-indented line exits 3.\n\nEvery function here is pure: it reads the original file text and returns ``TextEdit`` values.\nIndices are code points into ``SourceFile.text``. ruamel's end mark of a block scalar is not used;\nheader and content extents are re-derived from the text (see ``troupe.yamlcore``).\n\nChoices made where the contract is silent (TROUPE-002, TROUPE-008):\n\n- A value without ``\"\\\\n\"`` replacing an existing block scalar stays a block scalar with the same\n  indicator and ``strip`` chomping: the contract says a block scalar keeps its style.\n- A multi-line value replacing a plain or quoted scalar *inside a flow collection* is written\n  double-quoted: YAML does not allow block scalars in flow context.\n- A first content line led by a space (or a leading line of only spaces) gets an explicit\n  indentation indicator equal to the content indent minus the parent's column (the file's indent\n  step for new blocks); otherwise YAML would detect the wrong indentation.\n- Refused (``WriteRefused``, exit 3) because the result would not read back as the value, and no\n  other byte may chan...[truncated 26866 chars]

Tests also check that the untouched parts of the file are byte-identical.\n- The tests cover each contract sentence, the comment-column rule on the header line, CRLF, nested and sequence-item indents, the indentation indicator, `|-`/`|+`, and every folded case.\n- Two random tests are committed. One applies random literal and folded rewrites. The other edits 60 hand-written folded blocks containing more-indented lines, tabs, double spaces and trailing spaces. It checks that any more-indented line in the output is an unchanged original line.\n- Before the session was interrupted, a separate random test run (about 10k literal and 12.5k folded rewrites, LF and CRLF) had no failures. The only refusals were the expected "keep would absorb following blank lines" case. That script was lost when /tmp was wiped; the committed tests replace it.\n\n**Deviations, with reasons (all also written in the module docstring)**\n1. **Paragraphs are aligned with difflib, not matched only by position.** If they were matched by position, inserting one paragraph would re-wrap every paragraph after it. That breaks "unchanged paragraphs keep their bytes". Paragraphs inside a changed region are still paired by position to find their old width.\n2. **Folded values that already contain more-indented lines can still be edited.** The WP prompt suggested refusing in more cases. Instead I split folded values exactly the way YAML folds them: a more-indented line is its own unit and line breaks next to it are kept. A more-indented line is written only if it is an unchanged original one; anything else exits 3. Random tests check this against the YAML parser.\n3. **Some extra refusals (exit 3, message names the path)**, because the result would not read back correctly without changing other lines:\n   - `keep` chomping followed by blank lines, which would become part of the value.\n   - A following line indented as deep as the content, such as an indented comment.\n   - Text other than a comment after the scalar on its line.\n   - An indentation indicator above 9.\n4. **Signatures differ slightly from the WP prompt:**\n   - `literal_header` takes an `indent_step` argument (the indicator digit).\n   - `parse_block` takes an optional `indent_step`, used when the block has no content lines.\n   - `replace_literal` and `replace_folded` take a `path` argument for refusal messages.\n   - New public names: `choose_chomp`, `check_block_end`, `replace_block`, `replace_with_multiline`.\n5. **Choices where the contract is silent:**\n   - A value with no newline that replaces a block scalar stays a block, with `strip` chomping.\n   - A multi-line value replacing a scalar inside a flow collection is written double-quoted.\n   - Block body lines always end with a line break, even at end of file. Without it, a `clip` block at end of file loses the value's final newline.\n   - If the old folded value has no content lines, its longest-line width is 0, so new text gets one word per line. That follows the contract literally.\n\n**`blocks.py` public API for WP07/WP08**\n\nTypes: `Chomp = Literal["strip", "clip", "keep"]`, `BlockScalar` and `Paragraph` (frozen dataclasses), `DEFAULT_INDENT_STEP = 2`. `Context` comes from `scalars`.\n\n```python\ndef can_block(value: str) -> bool\ndef required_chomps(value: str) -> set[Chomp]\ndef choose_chomp(current: Chomp, value: str) -> Chomp\ndef literal_lines(value: str, content_indent: int) -> list[str]\ndef literal_header(value: str, chomp: Chomp, indent_step: int = 2) -> str\ndef render_new_multiline(value: str, context: Context, content_indent: int,\n                         indent_step: int = 2) -> tuple[str, list[str]]\ndef check_block_end(text: str, pos: int, content_indent: int, chomp: Chomp, path: str) -> None\ndef replace_with_multiline(text: str, start: int, end: int, value: str, *, context: Context,\n                           parent_indent: int, path: str, indent_step: int = 2,\n                           newline: str = "\n") -> list[TextEdit]\ndef parse_block(text: str, header_start: int, parent_indent: int,\n                indent_step: int = 2) -> BlockScalar\ndef replace_block(block: BlockScalar, text: str, new_value: str, newline: str,\n                  path: str) -> list[TextEdit]   # dispatches on | or >\ndef replace_literal(block, text, new_value, newline, path) -> list[TextEdit]\ndef replace_folded(block, text, new_value, newline, path) -> list[TextEdit]\ndef greedy_wrap(paragraph: str, width: int) -> list[str]\ndef folded_paragraphs(lines: list[str], content_indent: int) -> list[Paragraph]\n```\n\nWhat callers must pass:\n- **Block or flow context:** call `can_block(value)` first. If it is false, write a double-quoted scalar instead; the replace functions raise `ValueError` otherwise. In flow context, `replace_with_multiline` and `render_new_multiline` already return a double-quoted scalar.\n- **`parent_indent`:** the key's column, or the column of the `-` for a sequence item.\n- **`indent_step`:** the file's indent step from WP07, default 2.\n- **`newline`:** `fileio.detect_newline(text)`.\n- **`path`:** the dotted configuration path, used in `WriteRefused` messages.\n- **`replace_with_multiline`:** `start`/`end` must be a real plain or quoted scalar span. Empty or null values have unreliable zero-width spans, so don't pass those.\n- **`parse_block`:** `header_start` is the block scalar's span start (the index of `|` or `>`). Its end mark is ignored; the extents are worked out from the text.\n- **New keys (`render_new_multiline`):** in block context it returns `(header, body_lines)`. Write `key: <header>`, then each body line followed by `newline`. Content indent is `parent_indent + indent_step`. Call `check_block_end(text, insert_pos, content_indent, chomp, path)` on the insertion point to rule out absorbing the lines that follow; the chomp is `choose_chomp("clip", value)`.\n- **All edits:** returned edits never overlap and go straight to `apply_edits`. WP08 should still re-compose the final text before writing.
