The code still allows it when the more-indented lines stay unchanged (`replace_folded`, `src/troupe/writeback/blocks.py:437-443`, and deviation 2 in the module docstring). Re-reading each result through `troupe.yamlcore`, I confirmed three edits of `a: >\n  intro text\n\n    code line\n  outro\n` that go through but should be refused:\n- Changing the other paragraphs writes the file (`new intro`/`done`).\n- Removing the more-indented line writes the file.\n- Moving the more-indented line to the front writes the file with a `>2` header.\n\nRequired fix:\n- In `replace_folded`, refuse with `WriteRefused` (exit 3) when the old value has any more-indented line (`any(p.spaced for p in old)`), as well as when the new value would produce one. Do the check first. A write that changes nothing can still return no edits.\n- Update the module docstring to match.\n- Two existing tests expect these edits to succeed and must flip to expecting a refusal: `test_folded_unchanged_more_indented_line_keeps_its_bytes_when_others_change` and `test_folded_more_indented_line_moving_first_gets_an_indentation_indicator`.\n- Add tests for removing the more-indented line, and for an old line that is only extra spaces.\n- Check that the random folded-edit test still holds.\n\n**Recommended, not blocking (N1): a moved paragraph can lose its original line breaks.**\nSwapping two paragraphs re-wraps one of them even though its text did not change. For example, `six seven`/`eight` is rewritten as `six seven eight` on one line. That goes against "paragraphs whose text is unchanged keep their bytes". The fix is cheap: when a new paragraph's text matches an old, unused paragraph exactly, copy the old lines. Or document the current reading as deliberate.\n\n**The other deviations are acceptable:**\n- **Paragraph matching with difflib instead of by position:** better than the research note's position matching. Inserting or removing a paragraph leaves the paragraphs after it byte-identical; position matching would not. "Paragraph with no counterpart" is read correctly.\n- **Extra refusals:** keep chomping followed by blank lines, a deeper-indented line after the block, other text after the value on its line, and an indentation indicator over 9. All refuse rather than guess.\n- **End of file:** body lines always end with a line break, even at the end of the file.\n- **Single-line value replacing a block:** it stays a block with strip chomping.\n- **Empty old folded value:** width 0, so one word per line.\n\n**Adversarial cases that read back exactly (via `troupe.yamlcore`):**\n- **CRLF:** a plain value becoming a literal block keeps the header comment column.\n- **Nested indents:** keys at indent 4 with content at 6.\n- **Sequences:** sequence items and keys inside sequence items, including a space-led first line. The `|2` digit is correct relative to the dash or key column.\n- **Header comment column:** kept when chomping changes (`|-` to `|+`, and `|-` to `|`).\n- **Wrap boundary:** a line exactly as long as the width fits, and one character more wraps.\n- **End of file:** no final newline, with clip and with strip, plus `|+` at end of file.\n- **Line contents:** trailing spaces inside folded lines, and tab-led literal lines.\n- **Refusals:** a folded value with a tab- or space-led line is refused, and a following deeper-indented comment is refused.\n- **Other:** duplicate paragraphs work.\n\nI did not run move-task. I removed my temp files and the `__pycache__` folders; the untracked `.method/` directory was there before the review.

assistant: VERDICT: APPROVE\n\nRe-review of fix commit `bd80fac`, which only touches `src/troupe/writeback/blocks.py` and `tests/unit/test_blocks.py`. Both B1 and N1 are fixed, and none of my earlier adversarial cases regressed. One rare edge case remains and is worth a follow-up, but it doesn't block. No feedback-2 file was written.\n\n**Gates (lane-f worktree):**\n- `uv run pytest`: 775 passed\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: clean\n- `uv run ruff check`: clean\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: clean\n\n**B1 (more-indented lines), fixed:** the check now runs first in `replace_folded`, so any change to a folded value whose old content has a more-indented line is refused with exit 3. I checked each result by re-reading it through `troupe.yamlcore`:\n- Refused: changing another paragraph, removing the more-indented line, moving it to the front, a change to the chomping only, and an old line that is only extra spaces.\n- Writing back the identical value still returns no edits.\n- The module docstring and tests are updated: the two tests that expected success now expect a refusal, and there are new tests for removal, whitespace-only lines and the no-op.\n\n**N1 (reordered paragraphs), fixed:** a new paragraph whose text matches an old, unused paragraph now copies the old lines exactly. I checked a two-paragraph swap, a three-paragraph rotation, a duplicated new paragraph, and a CRLF swap; all keep their original bytes and read back exactly. A swap test was added as the orchestrator required.\n\n**Regressions:** none. The earlier cases all behave as before:\n- CRLF and header-comment column\n- nested indents, sequence items and keys inside sequence items (`|2`)\n- chomping changes\n- a line exactly as long as the width, and one character longer\n- end of file without a final newline, and `|+` at end of file\n- trailing spaces in folded lines, tab-led literal lines, refusing a tab-led folded line\n- the deep-comment refusal, duplicate paragraphs, an empty old folded value\n\n**Follow-up (non-blocking):** the new helper `_folded_value`, which recognises a no-op, disagrees with the parser on folded blocks whose content is only whitespace lines. I fuzzed 1,386 blocks with more-indented lines; all 73 mismatches had no real text, and every block with real text matched. The cause is in `parse_block`: when no line has text, it falls back to `parent_indent + indent_step`, so a line like `     ` counts as more-indented, while YAML reads it as empty. Two effects:\n1. `a: >\n     \n` (value `""`) set to `"x\ny\n"` is refused with exit 3, which is safe but not needed.\n2. Setting the same block to `"   \n"` returns no edits, as if nothing changed, even though the value is still `""`. Nothing gets corrupted, and WP08's planned re-read check should catch it.\n\nThe suggested fix is either to treat lines that are only spaces as empty when the block has no text line, or to have the caller pass in the parsed old value for the no-op comparison. I'd file this as a tracker for WP06 or WP08.\n\nI did not run move-task. I removed my temp files and the `__pycache__` folders; the untracked `.method/` directory was there before the review.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"New entries and collection values (FR-011, FR-012, FR-016, FR-019, FR-020; research R-06).\n\nContract:\n\n- FR-016: a new collection value is written in block style at the file's indentation (default 2),\n  or in flow style if its parent is a flow collection; replacing a scalar by a block collection\n  keeps the key's line bytes through its end-of-line comment. Product-owner ruling: a flow\n  collection under a block parent replaced by a new collection is written in *block* style.\n- FR-019: a new block list item or mapping key goes after the collection's last entry and below\n  any comment lines following it, before the next less-indented line; a comment line indented\n  less than the entries ends the collection. In a flow collection the new entry is appended after\n  the last entry, on that entry's line.\n- FR-015: a new key, and a new string value, copy the quoting style of the preceding sibling key\n  or value; with no sibling, plain or the minimal quoting the text needs.\n- FR-020: a changed line keeps its end-of-line comment at its column (``comments.replace_in_line``).\n\nEvery function is pure: it reads ``SourceFile.text`` and the SourceTree spans and returns\n``TextEdit`` values. Block collection ends reported by the parser run past trailing blank and\ncomment lines (see ``troupe.yamlcore``), so extents are derived here from the text: the last\nentry's content ends where its deepest last scalar ends (reliable spans), and the region rule\nbelow is applied line by line from there.\n\nChoices made where the contract is silent (TROUPE-002, TROUPE-008):\n\n- Indentation step (R-06): the first block mapping key or block sequence nested under a mapping\n  key deeper than that key gives the step; sequence items give no information (``- k: v`` is\n  compact form, not indentation). A new list under a key is flush (``key:`` then ``- a`` at the\n  key's column) if the nearest sibling block sequence in the same mapping is flush, else indented\n  by t...[truncated 35838 chars]

assistant: WP07 is finished and committed as `92d020b`. All four gates pass, and subtasks T030–T035 are marked done. As instructed, I did not run move-task, push, or touch `spec/`, and `__pycache__` is deleted.\n\n**Files** (only these two are committed):\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-g/src/troupe/writeback/collections.py`\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-g/tests/unit/test_collections.py`\n\n**Gate outputs**\n```\nuv run pytest             -> 936 passed in 12.78s\nuv run mypy --strict      -> Success: no issues found in 26 source files\nuv run ruff check         -> All checks passed!\nuv run ruff format --check -> 29 files already formatted\n```\n\n**Tests:** 161 tests in `test_collections.py`. Each named test asserts the exact full resulting text, re-loads it through `troupe.loader.load(..., overrides=...)`, and compares the whole configuration with the expected value. There are also two sweeps over four varied files, including BOM+CRLF, flow, compact lists and no final newline:\n- **New entries:** every mapping and list gets each of 9 value shapes. The test checks it re-loads correctly and that the bytes before and after the edit are identical.\n- **Kind changes:** 150 cases. 146 re-load correctly and 4 are refused. The refusals are correct: a following comment line would have become part of a new block scalar's text.\n\n**Bug found and fixed:** appending to a flow mapping whose last value is empty (`{a: }`) first produced `{a:, b: 1}`, which reads back with the key `a:`. It now writes `{a: , b: 1}`.\n\n**Deviations, with reasons**\n1. `Value` is declared in `collections.py` with the same shape as `troupe.values.Value`, because `values.py` (lane-d) isn't merged into lane-g. WP08 can import either; they are the same type.\n2. I added a keyword `path: str` to `new_key_edits`, `new_item_edits` and `replace_value_edits`, so that refusals name the config path (TROUPE-003). I also added an optional `seq_offset`, which implements R-06: a new list under a key is flush if the nearest sibling list in that mapping is flush.\n3. `render_block_value(value, indent, step, seq_offset=None)` returns lines without line breaks, so it has no `newline` parameter. `render_flow_value` takes an optional `preferred` style.\n4. **Open question for the product owner:** I applied FR-019 literally to flush lists. A column-0 comment after a flush list under a root key counts as "indented ≥ entries", so a new item goes below it (`tools:\n- read\n# section\n- write`). If the intent is that such a comment heads the next key, this needs a ruling.\n5. Choices recorded in the module docstring:\n   - Kind-changing replacement refuses with `WriteRefused` rather than drop a comment.\n   - Blank lines of a `|+`/`>+` value stay inside it.\n   - A file without a final newline keeps that, unless the inserted text ends with a block scalar.\n   - Collection-to-collection replacement keeps the old entry column where that column is valid.\n\n**Public API for WP08** (all pure; apply the result with `writeback.apply_edits`)\n```python\ntype Value = Scalar | dict[str, Value] | list[Value]\ndef detect_indent(text: str) -> int\ndef entry_column(text: str, node: MappingNode | SequenceNode) -> int\ndef insertion_point(text: str, node: MappingNode | SequenceNode) -> int\ndef render_block_value(value: Value, indent: int, step: int, seq_offset: int | None = None) -> list[str]\ndef render_flow_value(value: Value, preferred: QuoteStyle | None = None) -> str\ndef new_key_edits(file: SourceFile, mapping: MappingNode, keys: list[str], value: Value, step: int,\n                  *, path: str, seq_offset: int | None = None) -> list[TextEdit]\ndef new_item_edits(file: SourceFile, seq: SequenceNode, value: Value, step: int,\n                   *, path: str, seq_offset: int | None = None) -> list[TextEdit]\ndef flow_append_edits(file: SourceFile, node: MappingNode | SequenceNode, key: str | None,\n                      value: Value) -> list[TextEdit]\ndef replace_value_edits(file: SourceFile, parent: MappingNode | SequenceNode, index_or_key: str | int,\n                        old: Node, new_value: Value, step: int,\n                        *, path: str, seq_offset: int | None = None) -> list[TextEdit]\n```\n\nWhat callers must pass:\n- **`file`:** pass `node.origin.file`, i.e. the file that contains the parent collection.\n- **`step`:** pass `detect_indent(file.text)`.\n- **`new_key_edits`:** pass the deepest existing ancestor mapping. `keys` is the list of missing segments (at least one); intermediate mappings are created for all but the last. It handles block and flow mappings, and under a flow parent the intermediates become nested flow mappings.\n- **Appends:** `new_item_edits` covers an append (index == len) for block and flow lists.\n- **`replace_value_edits`:** only for kind changes: scalar or null ↔ collection, collection → collection, and flow → block under a block parent (per the ruling). Same-kind scalar changes stay with `scalars`/`blocks`. Pass the mapping key as a `str` or the list index as an `int`.\n\nInserted line breaks use the file's own newline. Callers should still re-load the new text to verify it, as WP08 plans.\n\n`WriteRefused` (exit 3, message names `path`) is raised when:\n- a new block scalar would absorb the lines after it;\n- removing an old value would drop a comment: a multi-line value containing `#`, or a comment after a value that starts on a line below its key.\n\n`ValueError` is raised only for programming errors, such as empty `keys`, or a key/index of the wrong type for the parent.

tool: Write {"content": "# WP07 review feedback (cycle 1): changes requested\n\nReviewed commit `92d020b` (`src/troupe/writeback/collections.py`, `tests/unit/test_collections.py`).\nGates are green: `uv run pytest` 936 passed, `mypy --strict` clean, `ruff check` clean,\n`ruff format --check` clean. Most of the work is solid. I ran about 110 adversarial cases, re-loaded\neach result with `loader.load(..., overrides=...)` and compared the whole configuration. Nearly all\nof them were correct and byte-minimal: nested and trailing comments at several indents, blank lines,\nBOM+CRLF, 4-space files, compact `- k: v`, multi-line flow, empty `[]`/`{}`, copying a sibling's\nquoting, intermediate mappings, scalar to collection and collection to scalar with and without\ncomments, and comment-column preservation.\n\nTwo defects remain. Each one changes the value of an **existing** entry that the user did not ask\nto change. The renderer produces those edits without raising an error. R-17 (verify before write)\nin WP08 would turn them into refusals, but the renderers must not depend on that safety net. Both\nfixes are small.\n\n## D1. Appending after a clip/keep block scalar at the end of a file with no final newline changes that scalar\n\n`_insert_entry` (and the same pattern in `_below_edits`) puts a line break before the new entry.\nWhen the file's last value is a `|`, `>`, `|+` or `>+` block scalar and the file has no final line\nbreak, that new break becomes the scalar's final line break. The scalar's value goes from `\"x\"` to\n`\"x\\n\"`. `|-`/`>-` are fine.\n\nReproductions (`new_key_edits` / `new_item_edits`, then re-load):\n\n| Input | Operation | Result | `s` before -> after |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `\"a:\\n  s: >\\n    x\"` | new key `a.t = 1` | `\"a:\\n  s: >\\n    x\\n  t: 1\"` | `'x'` -> `'x\\n'` |\n| `\"a:\\n  s: \\|+\\n    x\"` | new key `a.t = 1` | same shape | `'x'` -> `'x\\n'` |\n| `\"s: \\|\\n  x\"` | new root key `t = 1` | `\"s: \\|\\n  x\\nt: 1\"` | `'x'` -> `'x\\n'` |...[truncated 3286 chars]

assistant: VERDICT: REJECT\n\nAll four gates pass, but two edit paths change existing entries the user didn't ask to change. They do it silently, with no error. The fixes are small. Feedback is in `/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG/tasks/WP07-collections-and-new-entries/review-feedback-1.md`. I did not run move-task.\n\n**Gates (lane-g worktree, commit `92d020b`):** `uv run pytest` 936 passed; `mypy --strict` clean; `ruff check` clean; `ruff format --check` clean.\n\n**Adversarial testing:** I ran about 110 cases, re-loaded each result with `loader.load(..., overrides=...)`, compared the whole configuration and checked that bytes outside the edit were unchanged. Correct and minimal for:\n- nested and trailing comments at various indents, including the example from the WP prompt\n- blank lines before the next key, and files ending without a newline\n- BOM+CRLF and 4-space files\n- compact `- k: v` lists, `- - a`, and flow collections spread over several lines\n- empty `[]`/`{}`, and copying a sibling's double/single quoting\n- creating two levels of intermediate mappings\n- scalar↔collection and collection↔collection replacement with and without comments, keeping the comment column\n\n**Defects (in the feedback file):**\n1. **Appending at the end of a file with no final newline, when the last value is a `|`, `>`, `|+` or `>+` block scalar.** The line break added before the new entry becomes part of that scalar, so its value changes from `'x'` to `'x\n'`. Reproduced with:\n   - new key after `"a:\n  s: >\n    x"`\n   - new key after `"a:\n  s: |+\n    x"`\n   - new root key after `"s: |\n  x"`\n   - new list item after `"l:\n  - |\n    x"`\n\n   Suggested fix: raise `WriteRefused` here, since rewriting the header to `|-` would touch another line.\n2. **The flow-append `":"` check fires on any last entry whose text ends in `:`.** The `{a: }` fix in `flow_append_edits` (and the same check in `_replace_in_flow`) doesn't check whether the colon is inside the key or value. Results:\n   - `[C:, D:]` plus `E` gives `[C:, D: , E]`; the item `'D:'` becomes the mapping `{D: null}`.\n   - `{a:}` (key `'a:'`) plus a new key, or replacing that key's value, renames the key to `'a'`.\n   - `{a: b:}` plus a new key produces YAML that no longer parses.\n\nR-17 (verify before write, in WP08) would turn both into refusals instead of corrupted files. Still, these renderers shouldn't produce such edits in the first place, and WP08 isn't built yet.\n\n**The deviations you asked me to judge:**\n- **Accepted:**\n  - the local `Value` alias and the extra `path`/`seq_offset` parameters\n  - refusing a kind change rather than dropping a comment (safe and tested)\n  - blank lines at the end of a `|+`/`>+` value staying inside it\n  - handling of files without a final newline, apart from defect 1\n- **Flush-list rule:** this is the literal contract reading. A column-0 comment isn't indented less than column-0 entries, so the new item going below it follows FR-019, and it's documented.\n- **`{a: }` append fix:** the idea is right, but the check is too broad (defect 2).\n\n**Anti-pattern checklist:**\n1. Dead code: N/A. The only consumer is WP08's `apply_set`, so there is no production caller yet.\n2. Synthetic-fixture test: PASS.\n3. Silent empty return: PASS.\n4. FR coverage: PASS.\n5. Frozen surface: PASS (only the two owned files changed).\n6. Locked decision: PASS.\n\nI deleted my temp scripts in `/tmp` and the `__pycache__` folders created during the run. The untracked `.method/` was already in the worktree before the review.
assistant: The reviewer rejected WP07 for two real defects. Both silently change entries you didn't ask to change:\n1. **Block scalar at end of file:** appending after a block-scalar value at the end of a file with no final newline adds a newline to that value.\n2. **Over-broad colon check:** the "last entry ends in `:`" check in flow collections can turn a list item into a mapping, or rename a key.\n\nSending WP07 back for rework (cycle 1 of 3).

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-g && git add src/troupe/writeback/collections.py tests/unit/test_collections.py && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfix(WP07): never change existing values at an open file end or flow ':'\n\nReview cycle 1 found two ways an append silently changed an entry the\nuser did not touch:\n\n- Appending to a file that ends without a line break inside a |, >, |+\n  or >+ block scalar made the new break part of that value (\"x\" became\n  \"x\\n\"). Avoiding it would rewrite the header line, so such writes are\n  now refused with WriteRefused (checked by re-composing with the break).\n- The flow-append and flow-replace \":\" handling looked at the text before\n  the insertion point, so [C:, D:] turned 'D:' into a mapping, the key\n  'a:' in {a:} was renamed and {a: b:} stopped parsing. The empty-value\n  indicator is now taken from the parsed spans: a ':' after the key's\n  span of a flow mapping entry whose value is empty.\nEOF\ngit log -1 --oneline; method agent tasks mark-status T030 T031 T032 T033 T034 T035 --status done --mission troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG 2>&1 | tail -1; git status --short"}
assistant: Both review defects are fixed in commit `96f02e9`. All four gates pass, and T030–T035 are marked done again. As instructed, I did not run move-task, edit `spec/`, or push. Only `src/troupe/writeback/collections.py` and `tests/unit/test_collections.py` changed, and `__pycache__` is deleted.\n\n**D1: appending at the end of a file with no final newline, right after a block scalar**\n- **Fix:** the new-key/new-item path and the kind-change path that adds lines below a key or `-` now refuse with `WriteRefused` (exit 3) in this case. The message names the config path and the file. This only triggers when the text is about to get a line break added at its open end.\n- **How it decides:** it parses the text with and without the extra line break and compares every value. Any difference means the break would join the scalar's value. This covers `|`, `>`, `|+` and `>+` at any depth; `|-`/`>-` are unaffected.\n- **Tests:**\n  - All four reproductions from the review, plus `>+`, are refused.\n  - `|-` and `>-` still append correctly.\n  - The kind-change path at the end of a file after a block scalar (`s: |\n  x\nk:` and a list version) writes correctly and leaves the scalar unchanged.\n- The kind-change path below a key or `-` can't actually hit this case: the line being extended is the key or `-` line itself, never a scalar body. So its tests check that the scalar stays the same, not a refusal.\n\n**D2: the `:` check in flow collections**\n- **Fix:** it now uses the parsed structure instead of looking for `:` in the text. It applies only to a flow mapping entry whose value is empty and where a separate `:` follows the key's span. Lists never trigger it, and a `:` inside a key or value doesn't count. Both appending and replacing in flow collections use the same rule.\n- **Tests** (each re-loads the whole configuration):\n  - `[C:, D:]` + `E` gives `[C:, D:, E]`, and the comment keeps its column.\n  - `{a:}` + `c` gives `{a:, c: 1}`, and the key stays `a:`.\n  - `{x: 1, a:}` + `c` keeps the key `a:`.\n  - `{a: b:}` + `c` gives `{a: b:, c: 1}`, which still parses.\n  - Replacing the value of key `a:` in `{a:}` gives `{a:: [1]}`, which reads back as key `a:` with value `[1]`.\n  - `{a: }` still gives `{a: , b: 1}`.\n- One of my own cycle-1 tests expected `{a:}` + `b` to give `{a: , b: 1}`, which renamed the key. I removed that line; the new tests cover the case correctly.\n\n**Gate outputs**\n```\nuv run pytest              -> 948 passed in 8.96s\nuv run mypy --strict       -> Success: no issues found in 26 source files\nuv run ruff check          -> All checks passed!\nuv run ruff format --check -> 29 files already formatted\n```\n\nThe public API for WP08 hasn't changed, apart from the new `WriteRefused` case in D1. The module docstring now records both decisions.

assistant: VERDICT: APPROVE\n\nBoth cycle-1 defects are fixed in `96f02e9`, and none of my earlier cases regressed. I did not run move-task and did not write a review-feedback-2 file.\n\n**Gates (lane-g worktree):** `uv run pytest` 948 passed (936 before the fix); `mypy --strict` clean; `ruff check` clean; `ruff format --check` clean.\n\n**D1, appending at the end of a file with no final newline, after a block scalar: fixed.**\n- A new helper, `_check_open_end`, parses the file a second time with a newline added. If anything changes, it raises `WriteRefused`. It is called from `_insert_entry` and from the kind-change path that inserts lines below an anchor (`_below_edits`).\n- New key, root key and list item after `|`, `>`, `|+`, `>+` and `|2` are all refused. The CRLF version is refused too.\n- After `|-`, `>-` and `>2-` the append is still written, and the scalar keeps its value.\n- A kind change that writes below an anchor in front of such a scalar, a comment line after the scalar, and a whitespace-only last line are all written with every existing value unchanged.\n\n**D2, the flow `:` handling: fixed.** `_flow_entry_end` now reports whether there is a separate `:` after the key's span with an empty value, instead of looking at the character before the insertion point. Both `flow_append_edits` and `_replace_in_flow` use that.\n\n| Input | Operation | Result |\n|---|---|---|\n| `l: [C:, D:]  # drives` | append `E` | `l: [C:, D:, E] # drives` (items stay strings) |\n| `m: {a:}` (key `a:`) | new key `c` | `m: {a:, c: 1}` (key preserved) |\n| `m: {a:}` | replace the value of `a:` | `m: {a:: [1]}` (re-loads as key `a:` with `[1]`) |\n| `m: {a: b:}` | new key `c` | `m: {a: b:, c: 1}` (still parses) |\n| `m: {a: }` | new key `b` | `m: {a: , b: 1}` (still as before) |\n\n`{a : }`, `{"a":}` (append and replace), `{a}` and `{a:}` with a trailing comment are also correct.\n\n**Regression sweep:** I re-ran 96 of my cycle-1 cases. 93 re-load with every existing value unchanged. The other 3 are the documented refusals from cycle 1: a flow value over several lines with an inner comment, a value below its key followed by a comment, and a quoted value over several lines containing `#`. With the 52 new D1/D2 cases (36 correct, 16 expected refusals), that is 148 cases with no mismatches and no re-load failures.\n\nThe fix commit touches only the two owned files. It adds 8 tests for the D1/D2 cases.\n\n**Anti-pattern checklist:**\n1. Dead code: N/A. The only consumer is WP08's `apply_set`, so there is no production caller yet.\n2. Synthetic-fixture test: PASS.\n3. Silent empty return: PASS.\n4. FR coverage: PASS.\n5. Frozen surface: PASS.\n6. Locked decision: PASS.\n\nI removed my temp scripts in `/tmp/wp07rev` and the `__pycache__` folders. The untracked `.method/` was already in the worktree before the review.\n\nFiles reviewed:\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-g/src/troupe/writeback/collections.py`\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-g/tests/unit/test_collections.py`

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Where a ``set`` is written (FR-010..FR-013): the one place that decides it (TROUPE-007).\n\nContract (\"Where the change is written\"):\n\n- An existing value is changed in the file that contains it (FR-010). A scalar becomes\n  ``ReplaceScalar``, a mapping or list ``ReplaceCollection`` and a text include ``ReplaceText``\n  (the target file's content is rewritten; see ``troupe.writeback.apply``).\n- A value whose whole value comes from a YAML include is never replaced (FR-013, exit 3); a path\n  *inside* the included file is an ordinary path whose node belongs to the included file.\n- A new key goes into the file containing the **deepest existing ancestor mapping** of PATH,\n  creating the missing intermediate mappings there (FR-011). This rule is expected to change at\n  step 5 (C-005), which is why it lives here and nowhere else.\n- A final list index equal to the list's length appends; a larger index exits 5 (FR-012).\n\nChoices made where the contract is silent (TROUPE-002, TROUPE-008):\n\n- A key with an empty value (``key:``) holds a null scalar, not a mapping: a path through it is\n  a path through a scalar (exit 5), never a request to turn the null into a mapping.\n- Missing segments are only ever created as mapping keys. A digit-only missing segment below a\n  mapping would need either a mapping with a digit-only key (out of contract, C-004) or a new\n  list, which the contract does not describe, so it exits 5.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n\nfrom troupe.errors import PathError, WriteRefused\nfrom troupe.paths import is_index, unwrap, walk\nfrom troupe.tree import (\n    MappingNode,\n    Node,\n    ScalarNode,\n    SequenceNode,\n    TextInclude,\n    YamlInclude,\n)\nfrom troupe.writeback.scalars import Context\n\nCollection = MappingNode | SequenceNode\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ReplaceScalar:\n    \"\"\"Replace the existing scalar ``node`` at ``slot`` of ``parent``.\n\n    Attributes:\n        parent: The coll...[truncated 4355 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``apply_set``: plan, render and verify one ``set`` without touching the disk (FR-010..FR-020).\n\n``apply_set`` composes the building blocks: ``placement.place`` decides where the change goes,\n``scalars``/``comments``/``blocks``/``collections`` render ``TextEdit`` values, which are spliced\ninto the original texts. Before anything may be written, the new texts are re-loaded in memory\nand verified (research R-17): the value at PATH must read back as the new value, and the rest of\nthe configuration (every node outside PATH: kind, keys, order, values, scalar styles, includes)\nmust be unchanged. Any mismatch is a refusal (exit 3), so a renderer bug can never become a\ncorrupted file. A ``set`` whose value is already the current value is a no-op: no texts at all.\n\nHow an existing scalar is written (``ReplaceScalar``):\n\n- a single-line value replaces exactly the scalar's span, in its current quoting style if that\n  can represent it (``scalars.render_scalar``); the line's end-of-line comment keeps its column\n  (``comments.replace_in_line``);\n- a multi-line string replacing a plain or quoted scalar becomes a literal block, or a\n  double-quoted scalar inside a flow collection (``blocks.replace_with_multiline``);\n- a string replacing a literal or folded block scalar is written by ``blocks.replace_block``;\n- a collection value, a value for an empty (zero-width) null, and a non-string value replacing a\n  block scalar are kind changes (``collections.replace_value_edits``).\n\nText includes follow research R-16 (revised): with a CRLF target VALUE must be a string, written\nwith CRLF line endings (else exit 3); with an LF or break-free target a string is written as\nparsed and any non-string VALUE (scalar, mapping or list) as the VALUE argument exactly as typed.\n\nChoices made where the contract is silent (TROUPE-002, TROUPE-008):\n\n- A string a block scalar cannot hold (control characters other than tab) replacing a plain or\n  quoted scalar in...[truncated 13527 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/writeback/apply.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''\n\n# Imported late to keep the public names above readable in ``help()``.\nfrom collections.abc import Callable  # noqa: E402\nfrom typing import NoReturn  # noqa: E402\n\n__all__ = [\"WritePlan\", \"apply_set\"]\n_unused: Sequence[object] = ()\n''','\\n')\ns=s.replace('from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence\\n','from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping\\n')\ns=s.replace('from pathlib import Path\\n','from pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import NoReturn\\n')\ns=s.replace('''\n\ntype _Check = \"Callable[[Node], bool]\"\n''','')\ns=s.replace('reads_back: \"_Check\",','reads_back: Callable[[Node], bool],')\ns=s.replace('def _refuse(dotted: str) -> \"NoReturn\":','def _refuse(dotted: str) -> NoReturn:')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"_refuse\\|Callable\\|NoReturn\" src/troupe/writeback/apply.py"}
tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config set PATH VALUE``: change one value byte-surgically and record it (FR-010..FR-023).\n\nOrder (plan \"Engineering Alignment\"; research R-04, R-12, R-17):\n\n1. Parse PATH and VALUE: a usage error (exit 2) happens before anything is loaded or imported.\n2. Open the session: load the files on disk and import the theatre if this is its first command.\n3. ``apply_set``: decide placement, render, verify in memory. Refusals (exit 3) and path errors\n   (exit 5) happen here, before any write; a no-op returns no files.\n4. Write the changed file(s) atomically.\n5. Only after a successful write, append one event per changed leaf (``leaves.diff`` of the tree\n   before and the verified tree after). A failed write appends nothing.\n\nSuccess prints nothing.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\n\nfrom troupe.errors import WriteRefused\nfrom troupe.leaves import diff, flatten\nfrom troupe.paths import parse_path\nfrom troupe.session import open_session\nfrom troupe.theatre import Theatre\nfrom troupe.values import parse_value\nfrom troupe.writeback.apply import apply_set\nfrom troupe.writeback.fileio import write_files\n\n\ndef run(theatre: Theatre, args: argparse.Namespace) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Set ``args.path`` to ``args.value``; return the (empty) stdout text.\n\n    Raises:\n        UsageError: If PATH has an empty segment or VALUE is not one YAML flow node (exit 2).\n        LoadError: If the configuration cannot be loaded (exit 4).\n        WriteRefused: If the change cannot be written as contracted, or writing failed (exit 3).\n        PathError: If PATH cannot be reached or created (exit 5).\n    \"\"\"\n    path: str = args.path\n    raw_value: str = args.value\n    segments = parse_path(path)\n    value = parse_value(raw_value)\n    with open_session(theatre) as session:\n        plan = apply_set(session.tree, segments, path, value, raw_value)\n        if plan.tree is None:\n            return \"\"\n        changes = diff(flatten(session.tree), f...[truncated 449 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``set PATH VALUE`` on existing scalars, includes and errors, through the real CLI (US2).\n\nEvery test compares the complete bytes of every file of the theatre (history store excluded)\nafter the command: the changed file against its whole expected text, every other file against its\noriginal bytes (NFR-001). Refusals and errors must leave every byte unchanged (NFR-002) and print\nnothing on stdout. After a successful ``set``, ``show PATH`` prints the new value.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom conftest import HISTORY_PREFIX, CliResult, MakeTheatre, all_files, run_cli, snapshot\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"set\", path, value, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _show(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"show\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"history\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return [json.loads(line) for line in result.out.splitlines()]\n\n\ndef _bytes(files: Files) -> dict[str, bytes]:\n    return {\n        f\".troupe/{rel}\": content.encode(\"utf-8\") if isinstance(content, str) else content\n        for rel, content in files.items()\n    }\n\n\ndef _set_ok(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str, expected: Files, shown: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Run ``set``; assert success, the whole theatre's bytes, and ``show PATH``.\"\"\"\n    result = _set(theatre, path, value)\n    assert (result.code, result.out, result.err) == (0, \"\", \"\")\n    assert snapshot(theatre) == _bytes(expected)\n    assert _show(theatre, path) == shown\n\n\ndef _set_fails(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str, code: int, names: str) -> ...[truncated 16123 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``set`` adding keys and list items and changing a value's kind, through the real CLI.\n\nContract: FR-011 (deepest existing ancestor mapping, intermediate mappings), FR-012 (append),\nFR-015 (new keys/values copy the preceding sibling's quoting), FR-016 (new collections; block at\nthe file's indentation, flow under a flow parent; scalar to block collection keeps the key line),\nFR-019 (placement below trailing comments, above a less-indented line; flow append on the last\nentry's line). Every test compares the complete bytes of every theatre file; after a successful\n``set``, ``show`` reads the new leaf back.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, run_cli, snapshot\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"set\", path, value, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _show(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"show\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"history\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return [json.loads(line) for line in result.out.splitlines()]\n\n\ndef _bytes(files: Files) -> dict[str, bytes]:\n    return {\n        f\".troupe/{rel}\": content.encode(\"utf-8\") if isinstance(content, str) else content\n        for rel, content in files.items()\n    }\n\n\ndef _check(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n    files: Files,\n    path: str,\n    value: str,\n    expected: Files,\n    shown: dict[str, str],\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Build ``files``, run ``set``, assert every file's bytes and ``show`` of each leaf.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(files)\n    result = _set(theatre, path, value)\n    asse...[truncated 10584 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``set`` with multi-line strings: literal and folded block scalars, through the real CLI.\n\nContract: FR-017 (multi-line string replacing a plain/quoted scalar is a literal block, ``|-``\nwithout a trailing newline, double-quoted under a flow parent; an existing block scalar keeps its\nindicator and chomping unless the value's trailing newlines require another) and FR-018 (folded\nvalues: unchanged paragraphs keep their bytes, a changed paragraph is re-wrapped greedily at its\nold longest content-line width, an over-long word takes its own line, a new paragraph wraps at\nthe value's longest old content line, a more-indented line exits 3). Every test compares the\ncomplete bytes of every theatre file; after a successful ``set``, ``show`` prints the new value.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, run_cli, snapshot\n\nROOT = \".troupe/troupe.yml\"\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"set\", path, value, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _show(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"show\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"history\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return [json.loads(line) for line in result.out.splitlines()]\n\n\ndef _check(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, before: str | bytes, path: str, value: str, after: str | bytes\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"The theatre holds only the root file; assert its whole bytes and ``show PATH``.\n\n    ``value`` is the VALUE argument; ``show`` must print what it parses to.\n    \"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre({\"troupe.yml\": before})\n    result = _set(theatre, path, value)\n    assert ...[truncated 7309 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"History events recorded by ``set`` (FR-008, FR-022, FR-023, US3), through the real CLI.\n\nA ``set`` appends one ``set`` event per leaf it changes or creates and one ``remove`` event per\nleaf that no longer exists, after its write succeeded; ``old`` is whatever the file held at that\nmoment (hand edits included). No-op, refused and failed ``set`` commands append nothing. Every\ntest also compares the complete bytes of every theatre file.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, run_cli, snapshot\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\n\nFIXTURE: Files = {\n    \"troupe.yml\": (\n        \"model:\\n\"\n        \"  name: gpt\\n\"\n        \"  temperature: 0.2\\n\"\n        \"models: [x]\\n\"\n        \"tools: [a, b]\\n\"\n        \"agents:\\n\"\n        \"  coder: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\"\n    ),\n    \"agents/coder.yaml\": \"name: Ada\\n\",\n}\n\nIMPORT = [\n    {\"seq\": 1, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"model.name\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"gpt\"},\n    {\"seq\": 2, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"model.temperature\", \"old\": None, \"new\": 0.2},\n    {\"seq\": 3, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"models.0\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"x\"},\n    {\"seq\": 4, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"tools.0\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"a\"},\n    {\"seq\": 5, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"tools.1\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"b\"},\n    {\"seq\": 6, \"op\": \"import\", \"path\": \"agents.coder.name\", \"old\": None, \"new\": \"Ada\"},\n]\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"set\", path, value, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _set_ok(theatre: Path, path: str, value: str) -> None:\n    result = _set(theatre, path, value)\n    assert (result.code, result.out, result.err) == (0, \"\", \"\")\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path, *path: str) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"history\", *...[truncated 6823 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-h && cat >> tests/integration/test_set_existing.py <<'EOF'\n\n\n# --- Verify before write (research R-17) --------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_change_that_would_alter_another_path_is_refused_by_verification(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    # Both paths show the same file; rewriting it for one would change the other too.\n    theatre = make_theatre({\"troupe.yml\": \"a: file:p.md\\nb: file:p.md\\n\", \"p.md\": \"x\\n\"})\n    imported = _history(theatre)\n\n    result = _set_fails(theatre, \"a\", '\"y\\\\n\"', 3, \"a\")\n\n    assert \"byte-surgically\" in result.err\n    assert _history(theatre) == imported\n\n\ndef test_value_naming_a_missing_include_target_is_refused(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> None:\n    theatre = make_theatre({\"troupe.yml\": \"a: 1\\n\"})\n\n    result = _set_fails(theatre, \"b\", \"file:missing.md\", 3, \"b\")\n\n    assert \"byte-surgically\" in result.err\n\n\ndef test_value_naming_an_existing_text_file_becomes_an_include(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    theatre = make_theatre({\"troupe.yml\": \"a: 1\\n\", \"p.md\": \"hello\\n\"})\n\n    _set_ok(\n        theatre,\n        \"b\",\n        \"file:p.md\",\n        {\"troupe.yml\": \"a: 1\\nb: file:p.md\\n\", \"p.md\": \"hello\\n\"},\n        \"hello\\n\",\n    )\nEOF\nuv run pytest tests/integration/test_set_existing.py -q -k \"verification or include_target or existing_text\" 2>&1 | tail -3"}

assistant: WP08 is finished and committed in three chunks, and T036–T042 are marked done. The full suite passes (1185 tests), as do mypy, ruff check and ruff format. The quickstart produces exactly what its comments say. I did not run move-task, did not push, and did not touch `spec/` or any earlier WP's modules. I found no upstream defects, so there are no xfail tests.\n\n**Commits**\n- `6d06688` feat(WP08): set PATH VALUE with placement, verify-before-write and history\n- `69ad7de` feat(WP08): CLI integration tests for set with whole-file byte assertions\n- `8ce6656` feat(WP08): integration tests for verify-before-write refusals\n\n**Files** (all under `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-h/`)\n- `src/troupe/writeback/placement.py`: decides where a change is written. The "deepest existing ancestor mapping" rule (FR-011), which changes at step 5, lives only here.\n- `src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`: `apply_set(root, segments, dotted, value, raw_value) -> WritePlan(files, tree)`. It checks for a no-op before rendering anything. It then renders the edits and re-loads the new texts in memory. The write is refused (exit 3) unless the value at PATH reads back correctly and everything outside PATH is unchanged. "Unchanged" covers kinds, flow flags, keys and their order, values, quoting styles and include targets. The only allowed difference on the way to PATH is the new key or list item being added. The text-include rule is the revised R-16.\n- `src/troupe/commands/set_value.py`: steps run in this order:\n  1. Parse PATH and VALUE (exit 2 happens here, before anything is loaded).\n  2. Open the session.\n  3. Run `apply_set`.\n  4. Write the files atomically.\n  5. Record history events only after the write succeeds.\n\n  stdout is empty on success.\n- `tests/integration/test_set_existing.py` (47 tests), `test_set_new_entries.py` (27), `test_set_blocks.py` (18), `test_set_history.py` (11): every test runs the real CLI and compares the full bytes of every theatre file. Every successful case checks the new value with `show`. No-op tests also check that `st_mtime_ns` and the history are unchanged.\n\n**Gates (final run)**\n```\nuv run pytest              -> 1185 passed in 83.95s\nuv run mypy --strict       -> Success: no issues found in 44 source files\nuv run ruff check          -> All checks passed!\nuv run ruff format --check -> 47 files already formatted\n```\n`grep -rl ruamel src/` also lists `writeback/scalars.py` and `writeback/blocks.py`, but only because their comments mention the word. Searching for actual imports (`^(import|from) ruamel`) finds only `yamlcore.py`.\n\n**Quickstart:** I ran the steps in a mktemp directory rather than `/tmp/demo`. The outputs match its comments:\n- `show` prints `0.2`.\n- The temperature line becomes `temperature: 0.75`.\n- The tools line becomes `tools: [read, write, search]`.\n- `agents/coder.yaml` becomes `role: reviewer`.\n- `set agents.coder x` exits 3.\n- `history model` prints two import lines and then `{"seq": 6, "op": "set", ...}`.\n\nI couldn't delete that temp directory because `rm -rf` was denied; it is at `/tmp/tmp.YNBQFe5l3p`.\n\n**Deviations and choices where the contract is silent** (each is noted in the module docstrings):\n1. **Comment column after a shrink:** the orchestrator's case list said a comment "returns to its column" after shrinking back to `0.2`. Per FR-020, it returns to the column it had before *that* write, so the result is `temperature: 0.2     # creativity`. I renamed the test to say so.\n2. **Text the old value couldn't hold:**\n   - A non-string replacing a `|`/`>` block becomes plain (`text: 5`).\n   - A string a block scalar can't hold (control characters) is written through the same kind-change routine.\n   - A multi-line string with control characters in block context becomes double-quoted.\n3. **Missing list index:** a missing digit segment under a mapping (e.g. `model.extra.0`) exits 5. Creating it would need either a digit-only key (out of contract) or a new list, which the contract doesn't describe.\n4. **`file:` strings as values:** a VALUE such as `file:p.md` is checked as the include string that was written, so it is accepted. If the target is missing, the reload fails and the write is refused (exit 3).\n5. **Shared text include:** if one text-include file is used by two paths, setting one of them is refused (exit 3), because the other path would change too.\n6. **Replacing a mapping that contains includes** is allowed. The contract only refuses replacing a value that *is* an include.\n7. **A failed write (`OSError`)** becomes a write refusal (exit 3) that names the file, and no history event is recorded.
assistant: WP08 is implemented in three commits. The full suite passes (1,185 tests) and the quickstart works exactly as documented. Its choices on gaps in the contract are cautious: a text include shared by two paths is refused, and a failed write becomes exit 3 with nothing recorded. Moving WP08 to review.

tool: Bash {"command": "mkdir -p /tmp/rv8 && cd /tmp/rv8 && cat > snap.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, os, json, hashlib, subprocess, shutil\nW=\"/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-h\"\ndef snap(d):\n    out={}\n    for root,_,files in os.walk(d):\n        for f in files:\n            p=os.path.join(root,f)\n            if '/.troupe/' in p+'/' and (f.endswith('.db') or 'history' in f or f.endswith('-wal') or f.endswith('-shm') or f.endswith('.jsonl')) : continue\n            out[os.path.relpath(p,d)]=open(p,'rb').read()\n    return out\ndef run(d,*args):\n    r=subprocess.run([\"uv\",\"run\",\"--project\",W,\"troupe\",\"config\",*args,\"--theatre\",d],capture_output=True)\n    return r.returncode,r.stdout.decode(),r.stderr.decode()\ndef setv(d,path,val):\n    a=snap(d); rc,o,e=run(d,\"set\",path,val); b=snap(d)\n    print(f\"== set {path} {val!r} -> rc={rc} out={o!r} err={e.strip()!r}\")\n    for k in sorted(set(a)|set(b)):\n        if a.get(k)!=b.get(k):\n            print(f\"  -- {k}\\n    BEFORE: {a.get(k)!r}\\n    AFTER : {b.get(k)!r}\")\n    return rc\nEOF\nls /tmp/rv8"}

assistant: VERDICT: APPROVE\n\nWP08 is ready to move on. I found no contract violation, no ruling violation and no file corruption. All four quality gates pass.\n\n**Quality gates (run in the lane-h worktree)**\n- `uv run pytest`: 1185 passed.\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: no issues in 44 source files.\n- `uv run ruff check`: all checks passed.\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: 47 files already formatted.\n- The quickstart steps produce the documented output.\n- `ruamel` is imported only in `yamlcore.py`. `blocks.py` and `scalars.py` mention it only in comments.\n\n**Code (`set_value.py`, `apply.py`, `placement.py`)**\n- **Order:** PATH and VALUE are parsed before anything is loaded, so usage errors exit 2 and create no history file. Then load, then placement and rendering, then verification, then the atomic write, then history events.\n- **Verification can't be skipped:** every path that writes goes through `_verify`, including text includes. Only a no-op skips it, and a no-op writes nothing.\n- **Write failure:** an `OSError` while writing gives exit 3 naming the file, and no events are recorded.\n- **Placement:** all "where" decisions are in `placement.py`, as TROUPE-007 / C-005 require.\n\n**My own end-to-end testing through the real CLI**\nI ran about 80 `set` commands across 7 hand-written theatres, comparing every byte of every file before and after each one. The files included comments, blank lines, mixed quoting, nested includes in subdirectories, CRLF, BOM, 4-space indentation, flow collections spanning lines, block and folded scalars, emoji (comment columns are counted in code points), no newline at end of file, and comment-only or empty files.\n\n- **Matches the contract:**\n  - Comment columns, including the "value reaches the column → one space" case.\n  - Quoting kept or falling back in the right order (`'o''brien'`, `"it's \"x\""`).\n  - Literal blocks with `|` and `|-`, written with CRLF in CRLF files.\n  - Chomping changes on existing block scalars.\n  - Folded re-wrap at the old width with unchanged paragraphs kept byte-for-byte.\n  - New entries placed below trailing comments and above a less-indented comment.\n  - Flow appends on the last entry's line.\n  - A scalar replaced by a block collection keeps the key line through its comment.\n  - A flow list under a block parent replaced by a list becomes block style.\n  - New keys go into included files, with missing intermediate mappings created there.\n  - CRLF text includes: a string is converted to CRLF, a non-string exits 3. LF text includes write the VALUE argument exactly as typed.\n- **Refusals and errors change nothing:** exit 3 for a whole YAML include, a CRLF text include with a non-string VALUE, a folded value with a more-indented line, a shared text include, a `file:` target that doesn't exist, and a write failure. Exit 5 for `tools.2.name` past an append, an index beyond the length, a path below `key:`, and a missing digit segment. Every one of these leaves all bytes unchanged, prints nothing on stdout, names the path or file on stderr, and records no event.\n- **No-op:** `st_mtime_ns` is unchanged, including when only mapping key order differs.\n- **History:** events are correct across sequences of sets: changed leaves, new leaves, list shrinks producing `remove` events, hand edits taken as the next `old`, and `seq` continuing.\n\n**Implementer deviations: all acceptable**\n- The comment column is measured from the column before that particular write, which is the only reading possible when files on disk are the source of truth.\n- A non-string replacing a block scalar is written plain.\n- A missing digit segment under a mapping exits 5.\n- A `file:` VALUE is accepted only if the reload succeeds.\n- A shared text include exits 3.\n- Replacing a mapping that contains includes is allowed.\n- An `OSError` on write exits 3.\n\n**Non-blocking observations (no rejection)**\n1. The same refusal also applies, undisclosed, to a YAML include used at two paths: `set x.v 2` exits 3 when `x` and `y` both include `a/x.yaml`. This follows from R-17 ("every other leaf is unchanged"). The product owner may want to know shared YAML includes can't be edited, and the message ("cannot write this change byte-surgically") doesn't say why.\n2. No integration test covers the `OSError` → exit 3 path in `set_value.py`. I checked it by hand with a read-only include directory, and it behaved correctly.\n3. These come from code earlier work packages delivered, not WP08:\n   - If `.troupe/` itself is read-only, opening the SQLite history crashes with exit 1 and a traceback instead of a contracted exit code.\n   - A tab before a comment (`a: x\t# c`) is rejected at load with exit 4.\n   - A new key whose sibling is plain and whose value contains a tab is written single-quoted.\n\nNo feedback file was written because the verdict is approve. I made no source edits and ran no move-task. I removed the `__pycache__` directories I created; `git status` shows only the `.method/` folder that was already untracked before I started. My temporary test theatres are left in `/tmp/rv8/`.\n\nCode reviewed is in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG-lane-h/`:\n- `src/troupe/commands/set_value.py`\n- `src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`\n- `src/troupe/writeback/placement.py`\n- `tests/integration/test_set_existing.py`, `test_set_history.py`, `test_set_new_entries.py`, `test_set_blocks.py`

Recording the approval and filing the non-blocking observations as follow-up issues:\n- A YAML include used at two paths can't be edited, and the error message doesn't say why.\n- A read-only `.troupe/` folder crashes with a traceback instead of a contracted exit code.\n- No test covers the write-failure path.

Nothing was pushed and no pull request was opened.\n\n**On `main`:** `main` was fast-forwarded to `5426abb`, which adds two commits on top of the method bookkeeping:\n- `e0d74f9` is the code: 47 new source and test files.\n- `5426abb` adds Python build caches to `.gitignore`.\n\n**Verification on `main`:**\n- `uv run pytest`: 1,185 passed.\n- `uv run mypy --strict`, `uv run ruff check` and `uv run ruff format --check` are all clean.\n- An end-to-end run with `uv run troupe config …` from the repository root behaved as the contract says:\n  - `show` printed `0.2`.\n  - `set` changed only the value's bytes and kept the comment column.\n  - A CRLF text include was written back with CRLF.\n  - Replacing a whole YAML include exited 3.\n  - `history` printed the expected events.\n  - Only `.troupe/history.sqlite` was added.\n\n**How it was built:**\n- **Process:** all 8 work packages were implemented and reviewed independently. WP05, WP06 and WP07 each needed one rework cycle for defects the reviewers found:\n  - WP05 quoted some characters (emoji joiners, non-breaking spaces) that YAML allows plain.\n  - WP06 broke your folded-value ruling.\n  - WP07 could alter an existing value in two edge cases.\n- **Safety net:** before writing, `set` reloads the edited text in memory. If PATH doesn't hold the new value, or anything else changed, it refuses with exit 3 and changes no file.\n- **Decision records:** `research.md` holds my decisions and your rulings (R-13 to R-19). The acceptance matrix maps every requirement to the tests that prove it.\n\n**Where I worked around method 3.2.7:**\n- Its lane guards contradict each other about the `status.json` file, so I scripted a fix (helper scripts in `~/troupe-orch`).\n- Its automatic final squash would have deleted 15 planning files. I did that merge by hand, keeping the planning documents from the planning branch and the code, status and review records from the mission branch, then ran all four gates before committing.\n\n**Incident:** early on, a helper script merged a lane into `feat/troupe-config-load-show-set` by mistake. I undid it with `git reset --keep`, and the scripts now refuse to run outside a lane worktree. Nothing reached `main`.\n\n**Open follow-ups in tracker** (none breaks the contract):\n- `p3g` (P2): a read-only `.troupe/` folder crashes with a traceback instead of a contracted exit code.\n- `0uv`: an include under a folder that can't be read crashes with a traceback instead of exit 4.\n- `1wk`: a folded block containing only whitespace lines can be refused unnecessarily.\n- `b9b`: a YAML include used at two paths can't be edited, and the error doesn't say why. You may want to decide whether both paths should change.\n- `r55`: no automated test yet for a failed write exiting 3.\n\n**Housekeeping:**\n- **Checkout and branches:** the repository is now checked out on `main` (it started on `chore/project-charter`). The lane worktrees under `.worktrees/` (ignored by git) and the `method/mission-…` branches are kept, so you can delete them when you no longer need them.\n- **Backup:** an older generated `status.json` is backed up in `~/troupe-orch/untracked-backup`.

assistant: `method merge --mission troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG` **failed**. The delivered code is unaffected: `main` and `feat/troupe-config-load-show-set` both still point at `d0fa8b6`, the working tree is clean, no merge or revert is left in progress, and nothing was pushed.\n\n**Why it fails:** it squash-merges `method/mission-troupe-config-load-show-set-01M3XGPG` into `feat/troupe-config-load-show-set`. The coordination branch never carried the planning documents, so git sees `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `research.md`, `data-model.md` and `meta.json` as deleted on that side and modified on ours. That's the same failure I worked around earlier by squash-merging by hand in `e0d74f9`. So the merge method is trying to do is already done, and it will fail like this on every rerun.\n\n**One side effect:** to keep its state consistent after the failure, it reverted WP08's last status change on the coordination branch. WP08 now shows `approved`, and WP01–WP07 are still `done`. It also printed a "revert is already in progress" warning, but no worktree has a revert or merge pending; the only leftover is an `AUTO_MERGE` marker in the coordination worktree's git directory.\n\n**Decision for you:** what should happen to WP08's status? WP08's code is already on both branches (in `e0d74f9`), so I'd record it as done with a stated reason, using `method agent tasks move-task WP08 --to done --done-override-reason "Squash-merged manually in e0d74f9; method merge cannot merge planning files absent from the coordination branch"`. Otherwise I can leave the status as `approved`. I haven't changed anything, since you asked me to take the command's output as it stands.
